- **What inside an app does the work.** *"Identifying what works in mental health apps through meta-regression analyses of 169 trials"*, **npj Digital Medicine, 2026-03-11** (https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-026-02466-z). 169 trials, 340 groups, **N = 41,807**; 34 pre-registered "active elements" coded in both arms. Most strongly associated with **depression** improvement: **desensitisation, stimulus control, activity scheduling**; with **anxiety**: **exposure-based elements**. Explicitly *ineffective*: **graded tasks and personal strengths**. *"Interventions incorporating a greater number of elements were more effective."* Weighted dropout 28.0% experimental vs 23.7% comparator. **Most trials were in non-clinical (n = 71) or subclinical (n = 64) samples** — the app literature is largely a *subclinical* literature.
- **Real-world engagement is an order of magnitude worse than trial engagement.** Baumel A., Muench F., Edan S. & Kane J.M., *J Med Internet Res* 2019;21(9):e14567, DOI 10.2196/14567 (2019-09-25). 93 Android apps with ≥10,000 installs, independent panel data Aug 2017–Jul 2018: **median daily open rate 4.0%; median 15-day retention 3.9%; median 30-day retention 3.3%**; *">80% decline in app open rates between day 1 and day 10"*; breathing-exercise apps 0.0% median 30-day retention. ⚠ **This is 2019 data on a pre-LLM app market, and no equally independent post-2020 replication was found** — see [gaps](#11-known-gaps-and-unresolved-questions).
- **Harms are barely measured.** *"Systematic review and meta-analysis of adverse events in clinical trials of mental health apps"*, **npj Digital Medicine, 2024-12-18**, PROSPERO CRD42024506486. **Only 55 of 171 identified clinical trials (32%) reported adverse events at all.** Pooled deterioration across 13 app conditions **6.7% (4.3–10.1, I² = 75%)**; **deterioration did not differ between app and control arms (OR 0.79, 0.62–1.01, I² = 0%)**. Only 11 trials used an app that could alert a clinician. The authors: trials *"often provide insufficient information to properly judge risks related to app use"*.

### 6.4 NHS England: digital is embedded, and its recorded volume broke

**Composition of therapy, 2024-25** (*Additional analysis of therapy-based outcomes*, experimental statistics, https://digital.nhs.uk/data-and-information/publications/statistical/nhs-talking-therapies-for-anxiety-and-depression-annual-reports/2024-25/therapy-based-outcomes): **CBT was 49.8% of all courses of therapy**; **CBT + Guided Self Help (Book) together = 71.9–72.0% of courses**, up from 70.0% in 2023-24; 93.7% of courses delivered as discrete (not concurrent). **500,034 sessions of Internet Enabled Therapy (IET)** recorded, down 5.7% from 530,229 in 2023-24, itself down 16.6% from 635,759 in 2022-23.

⚠ **Do not read the 2026 monthly IET numbers as a collapse in use.** The March 2026 monthly release (published 2026-05-14) carries: *"**Guidance on the recording of Internet Enabled Therapies (IETs) changed in January 2026 and therefore may impact the results of measures relating to IETs**"*. Monthly IET sessions: Sep 2025 = 39,350; Oct 2025 = 39,076; Jan 2026 = 37,173; Feb 2026 = 34,767; **Mar 2026 = 16,681; Apr 2026 = 14,974; May 2026 = 15,610** (May release published 2026-07-09). Two readings follow and **only the first is safe**: (1) there is a **definitional break at January 2026**, not established evidence of a halving of digital delivery; (2) the post-break level has **stabilised rather than continued to fall** — May 2026 is *above* April 2026. Describe it as a level shift followed by a plateau. `?` NHS England's stated reliable-recovery targets (51% for 2026/27, 53% for 2028/29) are sourced only via a Nuffield Trust summary, not an NHS England planning document.

**Which digital products are actually permitted — two gates, both required: DTAC and an NHS England "digitally enabled therapies" (DET) assessment, on top of any NICE recommendation.**

- **NICE HTG675** (ex-HTE8), *Digitally enabled therapies for adults with depression: early value assessment*, published 2023-05-16, **last reviewed 14 January 2025**, **migrated HTE8 → HTG675 in December 2025** with recommendations unchanged (https://www.nice.org.uk/guidance/htg675 and /chapter/Update-information). Rec 1.1: **only Space from Depression (SilverCloud)**, with practitioner support in NHS Talking Therapies. **Beating the Blues** gone (*"As of February 2024, this technology is no longer available to the NHS"*); **Deprexis removed** (*"As of January 2025, this technology is no longer available in the UK"*). Research use only: Iona Mind, Minddistrict, Wysa. The original assessment rested on 46 papers / 32 studies, of which **only 14 RCTs**, mostly waitlist or usual-care controls. ⚠ **The guidance states it "will be reviewed within 4 years"** — published May 2023, so **review due by May 2027** — and **two of the three originally recommended depression products were removed for commercial withdrawal, not efficacy**. This is a supply-side snapshot of a churning market presented as a clinical recommendation set.
- **NICE HTG676** (ex-HTE9), anxiety: **five** technologies usable while evidence is generated — **iCT-PTSD (OxCADAT)** for PTSD, **iCT-SAD (OxCADAT)** for social anxiety, **Space from Anxiety (SilverCloud)** for GAD, **Perspectives (Koa Health)** for body dysmorphic disorder, **Spring (Cardiff University)** for PTSD (the last two additionally needing CE/UKCA marking). Research-only: Cerina, Iona Mind, Minddistrict, Resony, Wysa (GAD); Cerina, Minddistrict, Space from OCD (OCD); Minddistrict and SilverCloud programmes for health anxiety, panic, social anxiety and phobias. Verbatim condition: *"Low intensity interventions should be supported by a psychological wellbeing practitioner and high intensity interventions by a high intensity therapist."*
- **NICE HTG624** (ex-MTG70), **Sleepio**: *"**Sleepio is recommended as a cost saving option for treating insomnia and insomnia symptoms in primary care for people who would otherwise be offered sleep hygiene or sleeping pills.**"* Rec 1.3: **more research needed vs face-to-face CBT-I** — *"there is limited clinical evidence to show how effective Sleepio is compared with face-to-face CBT-I"*. Cost-saving at **£45 per person**, based on before/after primary-care resource use in 9 GP practices.
- **"CBT app" ≠ protocol-faithful CBT.** NHS England's *Digitally enabled therapies assessment criteria* page (https://www.england.nhs.uk/mental-health/adults/nhs-talking-therapies/digital/assessment-criteria/) — **six products assessed**. 2024 cohort: Space from Depression **fully compliant**; Beating the Blues **fully compliant**; Wysa (GAD) **partially compliant**; Mahana (IBS) **partially compliant**; **Deprexis NOT compliant** (*"primarily designed as a self-help, rather than a guided self-help programme… a substantial proportion of content which is not compliant"*); **Minddistrict (GAD) NOT compliant**. 2025 cohort: **SilverCloud Space from Anxiety fully compliant after the supplier was required to strip non-GAD content and add missing protocol elements**; **Koa Health iCT-SAD fully compliant, conditional on delivery with a high-intensity CBT therapist**. This is the sharpest available illustration of the point.
- **Digital front door ≠ digital therapy.** NIHR Journals Library, *Digital front door technologies to pre-assess people before assessment for NHS Talking Therapies [HTE10055]*, published **2026-06-25** (https://www.journalslibrary.nihr.ac.uk/hta/GJAB1822), *Health Technol Assess* 2026;30(52). Evidence existed for only **two** of four tools; the whole included evidence base was **two published peer-reviewed studies, six unpublished studies and five manufacturer RFI responses**. Limbic Access increased referrals vs non-adopting services, **OR 1.10 (1.075–1.131)**, with larger increases for Asian (1.29), Black (1.35) and non-binary (2.95) referrers; user satisfaction ≥89%; assessment duration reduced by 12.7 minutes. The gap the report flags: *"it is not known whether the increase in the number of referrals lead to an increase in the number of people completing a clinical assessment and starting treatment"*. `?` Two figures previously circulated — "used by ≈40% of NHS Talking Therapies" and a "15% vs 6%" before/after increase — could not be confirmed and should not be quoted.

### 6.5 US prescription digital therapeutics: the plumbing exists, the commercial layer keeps failing

**The device class.** *21 CFR 882.5801 — Computerized behavioral therapy device for psychiatric disorders*: Class II, prescription-only, product code PWE, *"intended to provide a computerized version of condition-specific behavioral therapy **as an adjunct to clinician supervised outpatient treatment**"* (https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-21/chapter-I/subchapter-H/part-882/subpart-F/section-882.5801), created by the classification order following De Novo **DEN160018** (reSET), *Federal Register* 2017-12-27. A parallel class **21 CFR 882.5803** for ADHD now exists.

**Reimbursement.** CMS created dedicated HCPCS codes for digital mental health treatment (DMHT) devices in the CY2025 Physician Fee Schedule and **expanded them in CY2026** (CMS-1832-F, issued 2025-10-31, 90 FR 49266 published 2025-11-05, effective 2026-01-01; MLN MM14315/CR 14315): **G0552** = device supply + initial education/onboarding per course of treatment (contractor-priced); **G0553/G0554** = ongoing treatment-management (nationally priced). For CY2026 CMS **expanded them to devices classified under 21 CFR 882.5803 (ADHD)**. Conditions: the billing practitioner must **incur the cost of the device as a supply**; the device must be furnished **incident to professional services in association with ongoing behavioral health treatment**; payment only for the **FDA-indicated use**. ⚠ A **CY2027 proposed rule (CMS-1848-P, RIN 0938-AV82)** was issued 2026-07-14 / published 2026-07-16 (91 FR 43842), comments to 2026-09-14, introducing a **"Software as a Medical Service" (SaMS)** category expressly distinct from prescription digital therapeutics. **CY2026 codes remain the operative rule for all of 2026.** `?` Whether CY2027 changes the DMHT codes themselves is not established — the 1,592-page rule was not read.

**Products.**
- **Rejoyn** (Otsuka Precision Health + Click Therapeutics, developed as CT-152): **FDA-cleared March 2024**, announced 2024-04-01/02; indication *"as an **adjunct** to clinician-managed outpatient care for adult patients with MDD **age 22 years and older who are on antidepressant medication**"*, explicitly *"not intended to be used as a standalone therapy or a substitute for medication"*. Six-week programme combining Emotional Faces Memory Task training with brief therapeutic lessons — **not a full CBT protocol**. **US launch 2024-08-14** at **$200 payer price / $50 discounted patient price**; **Great Britain launch 2025-06-17**, UKCA mark granted 2025-03-26, NHS DTAC certified.
- **SleepioRx** (Big Health): FDA 510(k) **2024-08-08**, insomnia disorder as an **adjunct to usual care**, adults 18+, 90-day CBT-I programme.
- **DaylightRx** (Big Health): FDA 510(k) **K233872**, decision **2024-08-30**, 21 CFR 882.5801, product code SCP, predicate **Somryst K191716**. Indication verbatim: *"Daylight is a prescription device delivering Cognitive Behavioral Therapy… intended to treat generalized anxiety disorder (GAD)… **as an adjunct to usual care** in patients aged 22 years and older."* (FDA clearance letter: https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/cdrh_docs/pdf23/K233872.pdf). **The strongest 2024–2026 efficacy signal for a self-directed digital CBT product against an active control:** Henry A.L., Emsley R., Miller C.B. et al., *JAMA Network Open* 2025;8(12):e2548884, NCT05748652 — single-blind decentralised RCT, **N = 351** adults with GAD-7 ≥15, comparator **active online psychoeducation**: GAD-7 **Cohen d = 1.09 (0.81–1.37) at week 10** and **0.96 (0.67–1.24) at week 24**; blinded-evaluator CGI-I remission **71.0% vs 34.6% at 10 weeks** and **77.7% vs 52.0% at 24 weeks**. Conflict note: several authors are Big Health employees. `?` The paper does **not** name Daylight/DaylightRx nor state it supported the 510(k) — the link is inference from authorship and timing.

**Read the labels literally: every FDA-cleared mental-health DTx in this class is an adjunct, none is authorised as standalone treatment, and each carries a minimum age (18 or 22) that excludes adolescents.**

**The commercial record is poor.** Pear Therapeutics — which created the regulatory category with reSET (2017), reSET-O and Somryst — **filed for Chapter 11 on 2023-04-07** (SEC Form 8-K, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1835567/000183556723000020/peartherapeuticsfilesforch.htm) and sold its assets at auction: Somryst to Nox Health ($3.9m), reSET/reSET-O to Harvest Bio ($2.03m), platform patents to Click Therapeutics ($70,000). **reSET, reSET-O and Somryst are not being prescribed or refilled.**

### 6.6 Generative AI: one landmark RCT, a contested control, no cleared product

**The landmark trial.** Heinz M.V. et al., *"Randomized Trial of a Generative AI Chatbot for Mental Health Treatment"*, **NEJM AI 2025**, DOI 10.1056/AIoa2400802, NCT06013137. **N = 210** adults with clinically significant MDD, GAD or clinically high risk for feeding/eating disorders; **4-week Therabot (n = 106) vs waitlist (n = 104)**. MDD **d = 0.845–0.903**; GAD **d = 0.794–0.840**; CHR-FED **d = 0.627–0.819** (note: Cohen's d derived from log-odds ratios, **not comparable to standard between-group d**). Average use > 6 hours; therapeutic alliance rated *"comparable to that of human therapists"*. Authors' own caveat: *"further research with larger clinical samples is needed"*.

**The methodological objection, in the same journal.** *"A Generative AI Chatbot for Mental Health Treatment: A Step in the Right Direction?"*, **NEJM AI**, DOI 10.1056/AIp2500453: *"**The Therabot group was compared with a wait-list control group, a likely nocebo.** Comparing Therabot with an attention-equivalent control group… would have increased the study's credibility. Comparing Therabot with 4 weeks of treatment provided by human therapists would have been ideal."* **Treat the Therabot effect sizes as an upper bound against no-treatment, not as evidence of parity with human therapy.**

**Where the pooled evidence sits.** Three meta-analyses in *npj Digital Medicine*, converging on *small*, with the most recent and most CBT-specific being the least favourable:
- **2026-03-25** (https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-026-02566-w), PROSPERO CRD42024598761, Jan 2017 – Oct 2025, 39 RCTs: **depression g = 0.31 (0.17–0.46); anxiety g = 0.28 (0.05–0.51)**.
- **2026-05-29** (https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-026-02820-1), **48 RCTs, 28,071 participants**: **SMD −0.27 (depression), −0.20 (anxiety), −0.26 (stress)**; risk of bias *"largely low"*; publication bias negligible.
- **Most recent, and the only one isolating CBT-based agents:** Hang, Wu, Feng, Yan, Liu, Xiao & Qiao, *"The effectiveness of CBT-based NLP-enabled AI conversational agents for mental health intervention"*, **npj Digital Medicine, 2026-06-12** (https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-026-02886-x). **15 RCTs, 1,737 participants**: a small-to-moderate effect on depressive symptoms and a small effect on negative affect, *"while the effects on **generalized anxiety, stress, and positive affect were not significant after adjusting for publication bias**"*. Counter-signal the authors flag: meta-regression found **higher-quality studies reported larger effects**.

> **Honest summary:** chatbot effect sizes sit in the same small band as the wider app literature (g ≈ 0.2–0.31), not the large band suggested by Therabot's waitlist-controlled numbers — and once narrowed to explicitly CBT-based AI agents and corrected for publication bias, **the anxiety and stress effects do not survive at all**. Any claim that "AI chatbots deliver CBT for anxiety" is, on current best evidence, unsupported.

**Commercial and regulatory reality, 2025–2026.**
- **Woebot** — the most-trialled rule-based CBT chatbot — **shut down its direct-to-consumer app on 2025-06-30**, the founder citing the cost of meeting FDA marketing-authorisation requirements and the absence of an FDA pathway for LLMs (STAT, 2025-07-02, https://www.statnews.com/2025/07/02/woebot-therapy-chatbot-shuts-down-founder-says-ai-moving-faster-than-regulators/). *Press source, used because no primary document exists.*
- **Ash** (Slingshot AI) launched **2025-07-22** with $93m raised; **withdrawn from the United Kingdom effective 2026-01-23** because *"there isn't a clear regulatory pathway for wellbeing products like ours"* (STAT, 2026-01-21; CEO Daniel Reid Cahn). Its published evidence is **not peer-reviewed**: a **single-arm observational cohort** of n = 1,284 new users with in-app single-item measures, within-person **d = 0.14–0.26** (arXiv 2606.28241, 2026-06-26, authors include Slingshot employees). A company safety paper (2026-01-21) reports 6.2% potentially-harmful responses vs 20.3–52.0% for GPT-5-series models — **self-published, self-evaluated, cite as a company claim only**. The UK backdrop: the **MHRA published guidance on digital mental health technologies on 2025-02-03** setting out which DMHTs qualify as Software as a Medical Device and how they are risk-classified.
- **No generative-AI mental-health device has been authorised.** The FDA's **Digital Health Advisory Committee met on 2025-11-06** on generative-AI-enabled digital mental health medical devices (docket **FDA-2025-N-2338**), discussing a **hypothetical prescription LLM therapy chatbot for adults with MDD**. `?` No FDA marketing authorisation for a generative-AI mental-health therapeutic was found.

**Regulatory and professional cautions.**
- **APA Health Advisory on the Use of Generative AI Chatbots and Wellness Applications for Mental Health**, released **2025-11-13** (https://www.apa.org/topics/artificial-intelligence-machine-learning/health-advisory-ai-chatbots-wellness-apps-mental-health.pdf). Recommendation 1 verbatim: *"**Do not rely on GenAI chatbots and wellness apps to deliver psychotherapy or psychological treatment.** GenAI chatbots, wellness apps that use GenAI, and digital wellness apps **should not be used as a replacement for a qualified mental health care provider, but may be appropriate as a supportive adjunct, not substitute, to an ongoing therapeutic relationship.**"* Policy asks include modernising regulations, evidence-based standards per tool category, addressing **gaps in FDA oversight**, and to *"**promote** legislation that prohibits AI chatbots from posing as licensed professionals"* (note: *promote*, not *enact* — an earlier corpus draft rendered this more forcefully). Scope note: the advisory **excludes** regulated digital therapeutics and traditional telehealth.
- **US states have legislated hard, and the 2025 "three states" picture is already stale.** **Illinois Public Act 104-0054** ("Wellness and Oversight for Psychological Resources Act", HB 1806, signed 2025-08-01, announced 2025-08-04) bars anyone from offering *"therapy or psychotherapy services, including through the use of Internet-based artificial intelligence"* unless conducted by a licensed professional, and bars licensed professionals from letting AI *"make independent therapeutic decisions… directly interact with clients in any form of therapeutic communication… generate therapeutic recommendations or treatment plans without review… or **detect emotions or mental states**"*; civil penalty **up to $10,000 per violation**. **Nevada AB406** (effective 2025-06-29) prohibits AI systems *"specifically programmed to provide a service or experience… that would constitute the practice of professional mental or behavioral health care"* and bars providers from using AI directly with patients. **Utah HB452** (effective 2025-05-07) requires disclosure that a mental-health chatbot is AI and bans selling/sharing user input. **2026 wave:** Maine LD 2082 (signed 2026-04-13, effective 2026-07-14); Colorado HB 1195 (effective 2026-08-12); Tennessee SB 1580 (effective 2026-07-01); Rhode Island S 2195/H 7350 (enacted 2026-06-22); California SB 243 companion-chatbot duties in force since 2026-01-01. **Still not law:** California SB 903, Pennsylvania HB 2100, New Jersey A 5603. `?` **The Maine, Colorado, Tennessee and Rhode Island statutory texts were not fetched** — enactment dates come from two trackers, unlike Illinois/Nevada/Utah whose primary texts were read.
- **FTC 6(b) study of AI companion chatbots**, orders announced **2025-09-11** to **Alphabet, Character Technologies, Instagram, Meta Platforms, OpenAI OpCo, Snap and X.AI**; Commission voted 3–0. It is a **study, not an enforcement action**. `?` The "resolution issued 10 September 2025" date and a "45-day response deadline" are not in the press release and were not verified.
- **Mechanism of harm is now in mainstream journals.** *"Technological folie à deux: feedback loops between AI chatbots and mental health"*, **Nature Mental Health, 2026-03-10** (https://www.nature.com/articles/s44220-026-00595-8): risk arises from interaction between human cognitive-emotional biases and *"companionship-reinforcing behaviors such as **sycophancy, role play and anthropomimesis**"*, with elevated risk in people whose conditions involve *"**altered belief-updating, reality-testing and social isolation**"*.
- **Pandemic-era flexibility is gone.** FDA's April 2020 *Enforcement Policy for Digital Health Devices For Treating Psychiatric Disorders* let computerised behavioural therapy devices be distributed without 510(k), registration, UDI or the 882.5801 special controls. Under the final transition plan (88 FR 18153, 2023-03-27) the List-1 guidances *"will no longer be in effect… **after November 7, 2023**"*.

### 6.7 Germany's DiGA — the largest reimbursed prescription-app system

Legal basis: the Digital Healthcare Act (DVG) in force **2019-12-19** created the "app on prescription" (§§ 33a, 139e SGB V); ~73 million statutory insureds are entitled to a DiGA prescribed by a **physician or psychotherapist**; BfArM fast-track assessment capped at three months; current fast-track guide **DiGA-Leitfaden version 3.6, dated 2025-12-10** (https://www.bfarm.de/EN/Medical-devices/Tasks/DiGA-and-DiPA/Digital-Health-Applications/Interesting-facts/_artikel.html).

**Portfolio — a 16 June 2025 snapshot, not current state.** Reported in *"Healthcare effects and evidence robustness of reimbursable digital health applications in Germany: a systematic review"*, **npj Digital Medicine, 2025-08-01** (https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-025-01879-6), citing the DiGA directory as of 2025-06-16: **44 permanent + 14 provisional listings; 11 delisted — six because no positive healthcare effect could be shown**, one because the study could not be completed, four at the manufacturer's request. Uptake and cost: **over 1 million prescriptions by end-2024, ~81% activated, €234 million of statutory-insurance spend 2020-09-01 → 2024-12-31; average €541 per prescription in 2024**, per-DiGA prices €119–€2,077. ⚠ **The DiGA directory is a live register with continuous listings and delistings — anyone quoting a portfolio size, delisting count or spend figure in 2026 must re-read the BfArM DiGA-Verzeichnis directly.**

**Quality of the evidence behind the listings.** Same review, PROSPERO CRD42023460497: all **23 identifiable approval studies for 21 DiGAs** published as of 2024-03-15; conducted 2012–2022; 56 to 1,245 participants. **13 of 23 had primary outcomes in mental health.** Dropout **mean 21.7% intervention vs 11.8% control**. Verbatim: *"**All reported significant medium to large effects, but risk of bias was high, particularly in outcome measurement (21/23) and due to missing data (15/23).**"* Conclusion: *"These findings raise concerns about the robustness of the evidence supporting DiGA efficacy."* Two earlier reviews reached the same high-RoB verdict, and **BfArM does not publish its own risk-of-bias assessments**.

---

## 7. France — the practical reality

**This area changes several times a year — five separate texts changed the reimbursement rules between June 2024 and June 2026.** Anything read elsewhere with "8 séances", "30 €/40 €" or "courrier d'adressage obligatoire" is out of date.

### 7.1 Who may legally practise

France regulates **titles**, not the **act** of psychotherapy. There is no protected act of *"psychothérapie"*; only *psychologue*, *psychothérapeute* and the medical specialty *psychiatre* are protected. This is the single most important structural fact of the French landscape, and it is why *"psychopraticien"* exists.

- **Psychologue** — protected by **article 44 of loi n° 85-772 du 25 juillet 1985** (https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/loda/article_lc/LEGIARTI000033678864), with mandatory free registration with the *agence régionale de santé*; usurping the title is a criminal offence under article 433-17 du code pénal. Implementing texts: décrets n° 90-255 and n° 90-259 du 22 mars 1990. **A psychologue is *not* a "profession de santé"** under the code de la santé publique — restated on the record by the ministry's own *délégation ministérielle à la santé mentale et à la psychiatrie*: « Nous ne conventionnons pas les psychologues car ce n'est pas une profession de santé, et qu'il s'agit d'une profession très hétérogène » (Sénat, rapport d'information n° 787 (2024-2025), déposé le 25 juin 2025, https://www.senat.fr/rap/r24-787/r24-78715.html). This underpins the entire "Mon soutien psy" design — a bespoke conventionnement, not a national convention like doctors'.
- **Registration moved from ADELI to RPPS in 2024** — psychologues in "lot 3" on **3 June 2024**, psychothérapeutes in "lot 4" on **11 October 2024** (ANS/esante.gouv.fr, page updated 2025-07-25). DREES: « Les dernières professions enregistrées dans ADELI […] ont basculé au RPPS en 2024. **ADELI est désormais décommissionné** ». **Head counts** (ANS, *Tableau des populations du RPPS*, version 25/09/2025): *psychologue* **114 172**; *psychothérapeute* **22 044**. ⚠ **The gap between 77 568 (ADELI, January 2024) and 114 172 (RPPS, 2025) is a repository artefact, not real growth** — RPPS captures salaried professionals ADELI missed, and de-registration was never enforced in ADELI. DREES itself says comparability work is ongoing. **Treat any "number of psychologists in France" claim with suspicion.**
- **Psychothérapeute** — **article 52 de la loi n° 2004-806 du 9 août 2004**, implemented by **décret n° 2010-534 du 20 mai 2010** (https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/loda/id/JORFTEXT000022244482), amended by décret n° 2012-695 du 7 mai 2012. Two cumulative conditions: registration on a departmental list held by the préfet after ARS instruction, and **validation of a training in *psychopathologie clinique* of at least 400 hours plus a practical internship of at least five months** in an establishment approved for four years. **Access to that training is restricted** to *docteurs en médecine*, holders of the *psychologue* title, and *psychanalystes* registered in their association's directories — **nobody can become a psychothérapeute in France without first being a doctor, a psychologist or a registered psychoanalyst.** Dispensations (annexe, read on the consolidated Légifrance text 2026-07-28): psychiatres **200 h, no stage**; other doctors **200 h, two months' stage**; **psychologues 200 h**, stage 0 or 2 months; psychanalystes **200 h, no stage**; everyone else the full **400 h + five months**. ⚠ An earlier corpus draft stated psychologues owe **zero** additional hours — that is wrong; only the *stage* can fall to zero. `?` The 0-vs-2-month stage cell for psychologues is the one to re-read before relying on it.
- **Psychiatre** — medical doctor with the **DES de psychiatrie**, extended **from four to five years**. **16 200 psychiatrists in activity at 1 January 2026** (DREES, https://drees.solidarites-sante.gouv.fr/communique-de-presse-jeux-de-donnees/jeux-de-donnees/260702-medecins-supplementaires-en-activite-en-France-au-1er-janvier-2026). ⚠ DREES does **not** rank psychiatry as "the third-largest specialty" — it lists psychiatrists among « les spécialistes les plus nombreux », behind a five-specialty *plateau médico-technique* group (16 600). Sénat n° 787: **15 582 psychiatrists at 1 January 2023**, 30% libéral / 48% hospital-salaried; hospital psychiatry headcount +17% 2010–2025 but **pédopsychiatrie −40%**; **one third of public psychiatry posts vacant**; density from **5.5 per 100 000 inhabitants (Indre) to 69 (Paris)**; **a quarter of departments have no pédopsychiatre at all**.
- **Psychopraticien** — **no legal status, no diploma requirement, no register, no supervision.** It emerged as a direct workaround after 2010: « Les personnes ne pouvant satisfaire aux obligations de la loi […] se sont bornées à effacer de leur plaque le mot de "psychothérapeute" pour le remplacer par celui de "psypraticien", de "psychopraticien" » (Sénat, rapport n° 480, 3 avril 2013, https://www.senat.fr/rap/r12-480-2/r12-480-213.html). The same source states the decisive legal point, still true in 2026: « **aujourd'hui, la pratique de la psychothérapie reste libre, s'exerçant dans un cadre réglementé comme non réglementé !** » **Practical consequence for a patient:** the only reliable public checks are the *Annuaire Santé* (annuaire.sante.fr, built on RPPS, listing psychologues and psychothérapeutes) and, for reimbursed sessions, the ameli directory of *psychologues partenaires*. **A "psychopraticien" appears in neither.**

### 7.2 TCC-specific training and certification

**There is no state-issued TCC qualification in France. Nothing in law mentions TCC.** What exists:

- **AFTCC** (founded 1971, association loi 1901) — « la première association de psychothérapeutes en France par le nombre de ses adhérents, **près de 2 500 membres** » (https://www.aftcc.org/qui-sommes-nous); EABCT lists **2 670 members**, president **Lucia Romo**. Publishes the *Journal of Behavioral and Cognitive Therapy*. **Base training** (https://www.aftcc.org/formation-en-tcc), three sequential modules over 3 years (or 2 in Paris): *Module 1 — Mise en place d'une TCC* **91 h in-person (13 days) + 17 h personal work**; *Module 2 — TCC de troubles spécifiques* **91 h (13 days) + 11 h + 3 h supervision**; *Module 3* **84 h (12 days) + 11 h + 3 h supervision**, including a mandatory 5-day *option au choix*. **Admission restricted to** psychologues, internes en psychiatrie, psychiatres and médecins non-psychiatres.
- **The "TP" label (Thérapeute Praticien en TCC)** — the closest thing France has to CBT certification (https://www.aftcc.org/therapeute-praticien-en-tcc). Granted to **psychologues and médecins who are AFTCC members**; requires « avoir une pratique régulière en TCC » and supervision by a TPS — **12 group sessions of 2 hours, or 12 hours of individual supervision**. **Validity: three years.** Renewal needs **≥4 continuing-education points per 3-year cycle**. **The label is closed to non-psychologists and non-doctors** — a "psychopraticien" cannot obtain it.
- **AFFORTHECC** (founded 1994, EABCT member since 1995, **EABCT-accredited in 2017**, fewer than 100 members, president Martine Bouvard).
- **University DU/DIU.** *DIU Thérapies comportementales et cognitives*, Université Claude Bernard Lyon 1 + Université Savoie Mont Blanc: **3 years, 34 modules, 175 h/year ≈ 525 h total**; admission for psychologues cliniciens, psychiatres, internes en psychiatrie and **infirmiers en psychiatrie institutionnelle**; fees €1 105 / €1 700 / €2 200. Note its own hedge: training « **proche des critères** de l'European Association of Behaviour and Cognitive Therapies (EABCT) » — not "accredited by". *DU Thérapies comportementales et cognitives*, Université Paris Cité: **300 hours over 2 academic years**; fees €1 770 standard + €300 dossier fee. **There is no national register or count of TCC DU/DIUs.**
- ⚠ **EABCT's own standards could not be quoted.** The EABCT Training Standards page (https://eabct.eu/training-and-accreditation/training-standards/) links only a PDF titled **"Training-and-Accreditation-2002-2013"**, which could not be decoded. **Do not quote hour counts for EABCT.** For scale comparison only (UK, not France): BABCP requires 450 hours of CBT-specific postgraduate training including ≥200 hours of direct structured teaching.

> **What a patient can actually verify.** Three signals: (1) the person holds the protected title *psychologue* or is a *psychiatre*/*médecin psychothérapeute* — check annuaire.sante.fr; (2) they hold the AFTCC **TP label**, time-limited to three years and requiring documented supervision; (3) they hold a university **DU/DIU de TCC**. **None of these is required by law to call oneself a "thérapeute TCC".**

### 7.3 "Mon soutien psy" — the current rules (verified 2026-07-28)

**Legal architecture.** Article **L. 162-58 du code de la sécurité sociale** (created by article 79 de la LFSS 2022, loi n° 2021-1754 du 23 décembre 2021), implemented by **décret n° 2022-195 du 17 février 2022** (articles R. 162-60 to R. 162-75 CSS). Tariffs and inclusion criteria: **arrêté du 8 mars 2022**. Convention type: **arrêté du 2 mars 2022**, wholly replaced by the **arrêté du 17 juin 2026**. The consolidated L. 162-58 on Légifrance is labelled **« Version en vigueur depuis le 14 juin 2026 au 1er octobre 2026 »** (https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000044577840).

**Sessions and tariff.** **Up to 12 sessions per calendar year, per patient: 1 *entretien d'évaluation* + up to 11 *séances de suivi*** (article R. 162-65 CSS; ameli assuré page updated **2026-02-06**, https://www.ameli.fr/assure/remboursements/rembourse/remboursement-seance-psychologue-mon-soutien-psy). **€50 per session for both**, billing codes **EEP** (entretien d'évaluation), **APS** (séance de suivi en présentiel), **PSS** (séance par vidéotransmission) — arrêté du 8 mars 2022, consolidated (https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/loda/id/LEGIARTI000045334523/). **Dépassement d'honoraires is forbidden** (L. 162-58 II); a non-compliant feuille de soins is rejected and returned to the patient, and the psychologist must reissue at €50 and refund the difference. ⚠ **A widely circulating error: "€50 for suivi and up to €60 for the entretien d'évaluation" is wrong** — the consolidated arrêté and ameli both give €50/€50.

**One statutory exception to the cap, since 13 June 2026.** **LOI n° 2026-492 du 12 juin 2026** (JO n° 137 du 13 juin 2026), article 13, adds a **III to L. 162-58**: « **Par dérogation au 1° du II, aucun plafond ne s'applique au nombre de séances d'accompagnement psychologique […] lorsque celles-ci sont prescrites à un mineur atteint d'une affection relevant des 3° et 4° de l'article L. 160-14 dans le cadre d'un protocole de soins mentionné à l'article L. 324-1.** » service-public.gouv.fr confirms in plain language: « Ce plafond a été supprimé pour les mineurs atteints d'une affection de longue durée, lorsque ces séances avec un psychologue s'inscrivent dans un protocole de soins » (https://www.service-public.gouv.fr/particuliers/actualites/A18968, 16 June 2026). **Two files in the research corpus state a flat cap "unchanged" as of 2026-07-28 — that is false.** See [§10](#10-where-this-dossiers-own-sources-disagree).

**Reimbursement split.** **60% Assurance Maladie obligatoire, 40% complémentaire santé**; **Alsace-Moselle 90%** (60% AMO + 30% régime local); **100% with tiers payant intégral** for beneficiaries of the *Complémentaire santé solidaire* or *Aide médicale de l'État*, for sessions linked to an **ALD** or an **accident du travail / maladie professionnelle**, and for **women from the 6th month of pregnancy**.

**No doctor's referral required since 15 June 2024.** « **Depuis le 15/06/2024, il n'est plus nécessaire d'être muni d'un courrier d'adressage** » (ameli psychologue). Operationally removed by the **arrêté du 24 juin 2024** modifying the convention type (deleting « adressés au moyen d'un courrier par leur médecin traitant », JO 28 June 2024); legally removed by **décret n° 2025-424 du 13 mai 2025**, which suppressed the 2° of article R. 162-64 and made the report to the médecin traitant subject to « accord du patient ». ⚠ **The 8 → 12 session increase and the removal of the adressage are two different reforms, eleven months apart** — the session count changed in May 2025 (décret n° 2025-424 + arrêté du 13 mai 2025, JO 15 May 2025, in force 16 May 2025); direct access dates from **June 2024**.

**Eligibility.** Age ≥ 3, « en souffrance psychique d'intensité légère à modérée », affiliated to a French social-security scheme or an AME beneficiary, seeing a conventioned psychologist. **Adults:** mild-to-moderate anxiety disorders; mild-to-moderate depressive disorders; tobacco, alcohol or cannabis misuse **excluding dependence**; eating disorders **without severity criteria**. **Children/adolescents (3–17):** « une situation de mal-être ou de souffrance psychique suscitant l'inquiétude de son entourage », with parental consent. **Exclusions (adults):** suicidal risk; severe depression or anxiety; severe eating disorders; severe neurodevelopmental disorders; **severe psychiatric history in the last 3 years**; substance **dependence**; **currently in ALD or invalidité for a psychiatric motive**; **sick leave >6 months for a psychiatric motive** — or any of these in the last 2 years (ameli psychologue, page updated 2025-10-08; service-public F34917, vérifié 2026-07-03).

> **Note the paradox:** an ALD *for a psychiatric motive* **excludes** you from Mon soutien psy, while an ALD *for a non-psychiatric motive* whose protocole de soins includes psychological support gives you **100% coverage with tiers payant**.

**Which psychologists can take part.** Article **R. 162-60 CSS**: registered with the ARS under article 44 of loi n° 85-772, **and** « disposer d'une expérience professionnelle en psychologie clinique ou en psychopathologie de **trois ans minimum** ». **The selecting authority changed in March 2026:** **décret n° 2026-163 du 4 mars 2026** (JO n° 56 du 6 mars 2026, in force 7 March 2026) updates articles D. 162-31 and D. 311-1 CSS so the competent authority is **no longer « la ministre en charge de la santé » but « le médecin conseil national auprès du directeur général de la CNAM »**, and grants supporting expert psychologists the status of *collaborateurs occasionnels du service public*. `?` **The Légifrance page for this décret would not open**; the number, date, JO reference and substance are corroborated by four independent records (Lexis Veille, Ascodocpsy/SantéPsy, the CH de Montfavet documentation catalogue, Tripalio) — **only the verbatim article text is unread**.

**Psychothérapeutes are excluded from the scheme.** Ministerial answer to a Sénat written question (Catherine Dumas, https://www.senat.fr/questions/base/2025/qSEQ250203198.html): « le dispositif n'est pas ouvert aux psychothérapeutes. Néanmoins […] le dispositif pourra à plus long terme être amplifié en ajoutant une "**seconde brique**" dédiée aux troubles plus sévères, et donc aux psychothérapies. »

**The new convention-cadre — arrêté du 17 juin 2026 (JORF n° 0142, 19 juin 2026, NOR SFHS2615340A).** It **replaces the entire annexe of the arrêté du 2 mars 2022** (https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/jorf/id/JORFTEXT000054273988). Verbatim, verified character-for-character on Légifrance 2026-07-28: « **Les parties rappellent qu'un psychologue conventionné ne peut pas réaliser son activité conventionnée exclusivement à distance.** À ce titre […] **la séance de bilan doit être réalisée en présentiel et qu'au maximum 20 % du volume de l'activité objet de la présente convention d'un psychologue peut être effectuée à distance**. Ce seuil est appliqué à l'activité annuelle globale du psychologue (et non par patient…). » And: « L'entretien d'évaluation doit nécessairement être réalisé en présentiel. Il est cependant possible de réaliser ensuite les séances de suivi à distance mais uniquement par vidéotransmission. » Breach is grounds for the sanction procedure at art. R. 162-65 CSS. ⚠ **Timing caveat: the cap does not bite uniformly from 19 June 2026** — the convention « entre en vigueur à compter de la date de sa signature par les parties et ce, pour une durée d'un an », so it binds each psychologist from *their* signature date, and it is a one-year instrument. Other content: mandatory tiers payant intégral for CSS/AME/pregnancy/ALD/AT-MP; payment within **7 working days**; resignation with 2 months' notice; **sessions performed after termination of the convention are not reimbursed**. `?` Operationally, every conventioned psychologist must sign and return the new convention or cease to be conventioned (AID-Psy-Co FAQ — **association source, not official**).

**Tiers payant generalisation from 1 October 2026.** « **1er octobre 2026 : tiers payant pour les consultations de psychologues conventionnés.** La loi du 30 décembre 2025 de financement de la Sécurité sociale pour 2026 généralise le tiers payant […] Cette généralisation est prévue pour le 1er octobre 2026. **Les mesures d'application seront précisées par un décret.** » — service-public.gouv.fr F34917, **vérifié le 3 juillet 2026** (https://www.service-public.gouv.fr/particuliers/vosdroits/F34917). The law is **LOI n° 2025-1403 du 30 décembre 2025** (JO n° 306 du 31 décembre 2025). Effect: the patient no longer advances the €30 AMO share, leaving a €20 out-of-pocket at the session, or €0 if the mutuelle also applies tiers payant. `?` **The LFSS 2026 article number carrying this measure could not be verified in the JO text** — several secondary sources say "article 42". The Légifrance version banner on L. 162-58 (« en vigueur […] au 1er octobre 2026 ») is consistent with a scheduled change on that date.

**LOI n° 2026-492 also creates two new reimbursement channels**, both requiring a *décret en Conseil d'État* taken **after HAS opinion** and therefore **not yet operational at 2026-07-28**: **article L. 162-64 CSS** — sessions by *ergothérapeutes, psychomotriciens, diététiciens* for minors in ALD on medical prescription within a protocole de soins; **article L. 162-65 CSS** — a reimbursed **bilan neuropsychologique** by a « psychologue spécialisé », requiring selection by a designated authority, a CPAM convention, medical prescription within a protocole de soins, and **accord préalable du service du contrôle médical**. The rapporteure noted such bilans currently cost « plusieurs centaines d'euros – parfois 500 euros » privately and are not reimbursed.

**Claims mechanics.** The psychologist completes a **feuille de soins**; the patient sends it to their caisse; reimbursement 60% AMO then 40% mutuelle. **The 12-session cap is systematically audited:** « Des contrôles sont réalisés systématiquement et des sommes d'argent pourront vous être demandées en cas de non-respect du nombre de séances prévues » (ameli, 2026-02-06). Changing psychologist mid-course is allowed but **the counter does not reset**.

### 7.4 Psychiatrist consultations and ALD

**A psychiatrist is a doctor; psychotherapy delivered by a psychiatrist is billed as a consultation.** There is no specific nomenclature act for psychotherapy — INSERM 2004 noted « les psychothérapies ne sont pas inscrites à la nomenclature des actes techniques, à l'exception des thérapies de groupe ». `?` No source showing this has changed was found; treat as probably still true but unverified for 2026.

**Tariffs at 1 January 2026** (ameli médecin, *Tarifs des médecins spécialistes en métropole*, header « Tarifs en euros au 1er janvier 2026 »): psychiatres/neuropsychiatres secteur 1 — **CNP €52.00** (consultation), VNP €52.00, **CP €42.50**, **APY €67.50** (avis ponctuel de consultant), **MP +€18.00** (majoration for patients up to 25). **Reimbursed 70%**, minus the **€2 participation forfaitaire** (which does not apply to under-18s, pregnant women from month 6, or CSS beneficiaries). **Direct access 16–25** without prior referral while remaining inside the parcours de soins coordonnés.

> **The €52 / €55 / €57 spread is a codes-vs-bundle artefact, not a contradiction — resolved 2026-07-28.** **€52 (CNP) is the bare act**; €52 + €18 = **€70** for a patient under 25 (which is ameli assuré's figure); CNAM's **€57** is the *consultation coordonnée* bundle and **€75** that bundle plus the under-25 majoration; service-public F34917's **€55 base / €36.50 reimbursed** is a third, intermediate bundling and is internally consistent (70% × 55 = €38.50 − €2 = €36.50). **All three are correct for different billing lines. Quote €52 only as the CNP, never as "the price of a psychiatrist consultation".** Secteur 2 non-OPTAM: free fees, reimbursed on a lower base (€13–€36.50) — the out-of-pocket is unbounded.

**CMP (centre médico-psychologique) consultations are free / fully covered** — the only route to fully-free psychotherapy in France, and also the saturated one (§7.6).

**ALD 23** (« affections psychiatriques de longue durée ») triggers **100% coverage on the tarif de responsabilité** for care defined in a *protocole de soins*. Psychotherapy **by a psychiatrist secteur 1** for an ALD-related condition is reimbursed at 100%. Psychotherapy **by a psychologue** is reimbursed **only** inside Mon soutien psy — and an ALD *for a psychiatric motive* is an exclusion criterion for that scheme.

⚠ **In January 2025 HAS removed psychotherapy from the ALD 23 acts list.** The *actes et prestations* documents for ALD 23 (both *Troubles anxieux graves* and *Troubles dépressifs récurrents ou persistants chez l'adulte*, adopted by the Collège 23 January 2025, online 31 January 2025) record in their own change logs **« Suppression de la psychothérapie »** from the reimbursable acts and **« Suppression du psychologue »** from the list of professionals. HAS states explicitly that these APALD documents are « **ni un outil d'aide à la décision clinique, ni une recommandation de bonne pratique** » — the removal is a reimbursement-scope statement, not a clinical downgrade of TCC. **It nevertheless means there is no current HAS document specifying TCC content or dose other than the June 2007 guide médecin.**

**Outside Mon soutien psy, psychologist sessions are not reimbursed by l'Assurance Maladie at all**; some mutuelles offer discretionary *forfaits*. **No acceptable primary source documenting typical forfait levels was found — do not quote figures from mutuelle marketing pages.**

### 7.5 Where TCC actually appears in French guidelines

France's guideline stock is **much thinner and much older** than NICE's, and HAS typically lists TCC alongside other structured psychotherapies rather than as a preferred modality — with two clear exceptions.

- **Depression (adults)** — HAS RBP October 2017 (see §3.1). **TCC is named first but not preferred.** Still current at 2026-07-28; the only 2026 HAS depression output is *Avis n° 2026.0026/AC/SBP du 23 avril 2026* (online 4 May 2026) adopting *référentiels de prescription d'arrêt de travail* — an administrative document that does not touch psychotherapy choice. **HAS depression treatment guidance has not been revised in nearly nine years.**
- **Chronic insomnia — TCC is unambiguously first-line.** HAS/SFTG RPC *Prise en charge du patient adulte se plaignant d'insomnie en médecine générale*, validated **20 December 2006**, online 3 May 2007: « le traitement préférentiel de l'insomnie en première intention est, dans la mesure du possible, une **thérapie comportementale** ou une psychothérapie », with stimulus control and sleep restriction « parmi les techniques les plus efficaces ». **The same 2006 document already flagged the French access problem:** « En France, ces méthodes thérapeutiques sont **peu développées, voire méconnues**. […] **l'accessibilité à ces traitements est extrêmement limitée**. » Twenty years later, Mon soutien psy is the first mechanism that partially solvabilises it. Restated in 2025: the **HAS CNEDiMTS avis on SOMNIO** (adopted 15 July 2025, published 25 September 2025) says « Les recommandations de bonnes pratiques […] **placent toutes les thérapies cognitivo-comportementales en première ligne de traitement** pour la prise en charge des symptômes de l'insomnie chronique. » And the HAS Commission de la Transparence avis on QUVIVIQ restates that « **aucun autre traitement hypnotique n'est recommandé** dans le traitement de l'insomnie chronique ».
- **⚠ But no digital TCC-I is reimbursed in France.** **SOMNIO's *service attendu* was judged INSUFFICIENT** for LPPR inscription (« Compte tenu du faible niveau de preuve des données fournies, l'intérêt de SOMNIO […] ne peut être établi » — pivotal RCT with ~30% missing data; re-verified on the HAS product page 2026-07-28). **HelloBetter Insomnie** received an *avis défavorable* for *prise en charge anticipée numérique* (PECAN), online 2 August 2024 — the first PECAN refusal for a digital therapeutic in France. `?` **"No reimbursed digital TCC-I in France as of 2026-07-28" is a negative claim verified only against these two dossiers and the two reimbursement routes (LPPR and PECAN)** — it is not proof that no other product has since been listed.
- **TDAH (children/adolescents)** — HAS RBP validated **18 July 2024**, online 23 September 2024 (https://www.has-sante.fr/jcms/p_3302482/). Non-drug interventions listed as « **Psychoéducation, PEHP (Programmes d'Entraînement aux Habiletés Parentales), place des TCCE (thérapies comportementales, cognitives et émotionnelles)** »; drug treatment « **En première intention, méthylphénidate (MPH) LP** […] **En deuxième intention, atomoxétine** », « systématiquement associée à des mesures non médicamenteuses ». ⚠ **Do not attribute a grade to any TDAH intervention from this dossier** — an earlier corpus draft attached « grade B » to psychoéducation and said it was recommended systematically; neither appears in any readable HAS source, and the HAS Q/R (22 January 2025) restricts TCCE further to « **dans certains cas** les TCCE ». **The adult TDAH recommendation is still pending** (note de cadrage only).
- **Autism** — HAS RBP *Trouble du spectre de l'autisme*, adopted **8 janvier 2026**, online **12 février 2026** (https://www.has-sante.fr/jcms/p_3858412/). **Recommended:** early developmental and behavioural interventions from the first warning signs, without waiting for diagnostic confirmation; for infants and children at established risk, « **au moins 10 heures hebdomadaires** d'interventions ». **Explicitly *non recommandées*, in three tiers** — *absence of studies*: Doman-Delacato, Feuerstein, Padovan, communication facilitée / Rapid Prompting Method, Saccade®; *insufficient level of evidence*: intégration des réflexes archaïques, Son-Rise®, 3i, **neurofeedback**, « **psychanalyse et autres interventions basées sur des approches psychanalytiques** », Snoezelen; *studies proving absence of effect*: auditory-integration practices including **méthode Tomatis** and **packing**. The three-tier structure and list membership are independently confirmed by the HAS press release of 12 February 2026. `?` The **per-item tier assignment** (psychanalyse in *preuves insuffisantes*, packing in *inefficaces*) rests on a single read of §3.1.3 of the recommendation PDF, not on the press release, which does not split them.
- **Tinnitus — the newest French guidance naming TCC.** HAS RBP *Acouphènes invalidants chez l'adulte*, published **16 juillet 2026** (https://www.has-sante.fr/jcms/p_4159100/): « La prise en charge thérapeutique est graduée et personnalisée […] : **les thérapies cognitivo-comportementales sont recommandées dans tous les cas** ».
- **⚠ There is no HAS PTSD guideline at all.** The *note de cadrage* « Évaluation et prise en charge des syndromes psychotraumatiques — Enfants et adultes » was **validée par le Collège le 14 octobre 2020**, off a *saisine* dated 1 June 2017, and set its own target of Collège validation in **November 2021**. That target was missed by ~5 years; the March 2026 programme re-issue still describes the adult work as « en cours d'élaboration … (note de cadrage) ». The cadrage itself states why: « **il n'existe pas de recommandations de bonnes pratiques cliniques pour le diagnostic, l'évaluation et la prise en charge des syndromes psychotraumatiques disponibles en langue française** ». **French clinicians citing "les recommandations HAS sur le TSPT" are citing something that does not exist**; the reference standards in France are NICE NG116, APA 2025 and VA/DoD 2023. `?` A September 2025 HAS slide deck listed the PTSD RBP for "S1 2026"; H1 2026 has passed with no publication, and the only copy found is on a third-party site.
- **⚠ There is no current HAS recommendation on adult anxiety disorders.** The only HAS-branded document is the **June 2007 ALD guide** *Troubles anxieux graves*. **Treat "la HAS recommande la TCC en première intention pour les troubles anxieux" as UNSOURCED for 2026.**
- **The 2007 ALD 23 guide médecin is the only French numeric dose anchor in existence**, and it is historical. Validated by the Collège de la HAS in **June 2007** (https://www.has-sante.fr/upload/docs/application/pdf/guide_medecin_troubles_anxieux.pdf), every quotation below re-read from the HAS PDF and confirmed verbatim 2026-07-28: **GAD** — « la durée optimale est de **12 à 25 séances de 45 minutes** environ »; **trouble panique** — « La thérapie cognitivo comportementale comporte dans la plupart des cas **12 à 25 séances de 45 minutes** environ », and « L'association traitement médicamenteux et thérapie cognitivo-comportementale n'est pas recommandée à la phase initiale, sauf dans les formes sévères ou résistantes »; **TOC** — « Les Thérapies cognitives et comportementales (TCC) sont le traitement de choix [basées sur l'exposition avec prévention de la réponse (EPR)]. Dans la plupart des cas, elles reposent sur **au moins 25 séances de 45 minutes** environ », with TCC-seule failure defined as « au moins **20 à 40 séances** de 45 minutes environ ». It also codifies *gestion de l'anxiété par soi-même* + bibliothérapie as a distinct low-intensity option, noting « **Le contact avec le thérapeute reste indispensable.** » **This document predates the entire inhibitory-learning literature, the 2016–2019 safety-behaviour work, NG222/NG116/CG159, the Unified Protocol equivalence trial and every meta-analysis in this dossier.**

### 7.6 Access

**Mon soutien psy uptake — and a live data inconsistency the reader must not resolve.**

| Date | Patients (cumulative) | Sessions | Conventioned psychologists | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 28 Feb 2025 | 586 858 | 3.1 M (avg 4.7/beneficiary) | 5 217 (« 15 % des psychologues libéraux ») | Sénat n° 787, 25 June 2025 |
| ~1 Apr 2025 | 587 000 | — | ~5 500 | info.gouv.fr (also reported via a CNAM press briefing — **pointer**) |
| May 2026 | « plus de 1 M patients (dont 461 756 en 2025) » | 5.9 M | **7 799** | DMSMP, *État des lieux de la GCN 2025*, June 2026 |
| 2 June 2026 | « **près de 1,8 million** de patients accompagnés depuis sa création » ; annual beneficiaries **109 798 (2022) → 519 051 (2025)** | — | — | CP ministériel, 2 juin 2026 |

⚠ **The two June-2026 government documents disagree with each other** — « plus de 1 M » vs « près de 1,8 million », and 461 756 vs 519 051 beneficiaries for 2025 — released the same week by the same ministry. **Do not present either as *the* figure.** The ×5 growth statement (109 798 → 519 051) is the most specific and internally consistent claim. Separately, **a "6,200+ participating psychologists" figure appears only in secondary press/aggregator pages and is rejected as unsourced**; the sourced series is 5 217 (Feb 2025) → ~5 500 (Apr 2025) → 7 799 (May 2026), with a pro-engagement association putting it at « environ 8 000 » in April 2026.

**Adhesion is the weak point.** Sénat n° 787: « le dispositif ne mobilise que **15 %** des psychologues libéraux et certains territoires sont très faiblement couverts », and « il existe un risque sérieux de rupture de la prise en charge du patient **au bout des douze séances remboursées** ». The rapporteure adds that « les représentants des psychologues ne sont pas unanimes […] Certains estiment que l'argent qui lui est consacré, **660 millions d'euros sur la période 2022-2026**, devrait revenir prioritairement aux services publics exsangues ». **Ministry view, same hearings:** « il s'agit d'un **dispositif de première ligne** […] le nombre moyen de séances atteint **4,8**. Ce nombre limité montre que le dispositif permet de répondre aux situations concernées. Pour autant, le dispositif n'a pas vocation à répondre aux cas de personnes souffrant d'un trouble psychique envahissant. »

**Waiting times — CMP.** Sénat n° 787 (25 June 2025): waiting times « **jusqu'à 6 mois**, par exemple, dans les Pays de la Loire » (the report's own example reads as 4–6 months); **2 934 CMP** in 2023 (1 630 adultes, 1 304 infanto-juvéniles); a **€56 M** envelope announced in 2021 to recruit 800 non-medical ETP into CMPs, hampered by recruitment failures; and « Loin de leur logique de prise en charge universelle, les CMP sont contraints de réaliser un **tri entre les patients**, en ciblant les situations urgentes. » **ARS Île-de-France regional survey** (June 2024, **348 structures, 94% participation**, https://www.iledefrance.ars.sante.fr/une-enquete-regionale-sur-les-centres-medico-psychologiques-cmp): **adult CMPs — between 9% and 42%** of structures, depending on department, cannot offer a medical appointment within 3 months, with psychologist consultations showing longer delays still; **child/adolescent CMPs — only 30%** achieve an appointment in under 3 months, and departments **77, 91, 93 and 95** report waits **exceeding one year**; **fewer than two thirds** offer evening or weekend slots.

**Waiting times — private practice: there are none published.** DREES's *Enquête Délais d'attente* — the only public statistical instrument on appointment delays — **explicitly excludes psychologists**: « L'enquête exclut […] les demandes de rendez-vous auprès d'un auxiliaire médical […] **ou d'un psychologue** » (https://drees.solidarites-sante.gouv.fr/sources-outils-et-enquetes/lenquete-delais-dattente). Its published results cover GPs, dentists, ophthalmologists, gynaecologists, paediatricians, dermatologists, cardiologists, rheumatologists and radiologists — **not psychiatrists (sample too small), and not psychologists**. `?` The only quantified recent series for psychiatrists is a **Doctolib × Fondation Jean Jaurès** study (2nd edition, 19 May 2026, 234 million 2025 appointments) reporting lengthening delays — **a private-company dataset restricted to Doctolib bookings, not an acceptable primary source**, and the psychiatrist median was not stated in reachable coverage.

**National policy context.** *Santé mentale* was **Grande cause nationale 2025** (announced 11 October 2024), **extended into 2026** with new axes. The **Feuille de route « santé mentale et psychiatrie »** (since 2018; 3 pillars, 54 actions) was enriched by the June 2025 ministerial plan. 2025 results relevant to access (DMSMP deck, June 2026; CP 2 June 2026): **300 000 people trained in premiers secours en santé mentale** by early 2026, a year ahead of target, and CPF-eligible from rentrée 2026; **125 Maisons des adolescents** covering every department, funding raised from €5 M to **€15 M**; **56 filières psychiatriques** within the Service d'accès aux soins covering 58 departments; the **3114** suicide-prevention line handled **549 123 calls** (+62% vs 2024); **VigilanS** covers 100 departments; ONDAM psychiatrie **+42% from 2020 to 2024**. **2026 measures:** from *rentrée 2026*, a national **priority-orientation mechanism** for pupils identified in psychological distress; a national mapping of mental-health resources; a **« zéro contention » target by 2030**; and a new **délégation interministérielle à la santé mentale et à la psychiatrie**. **HAS's programme pluriannuel 2025-2030** (adopted 14 February 2025, 9 themes) has pending TCC-relevant items: **TDAH adulte**, **troubles dys**, **trouble de la personnalité limite**, and **évaluation et prise en charge du trouble de stress post-traumatique**. **Collaborative-care experiments** (GP–psychologist–psychiatrist) exist — **Sésame** (Île-de-France), **DSPP** (Occitanie), **MG&PSY37** (Centre-Val de Loire) — with a 2025 working group to decide a national model. This is the most likely future route to guideline-concordant, longer psychotherapy in French primary care.

### 7.7 French-specific controversies

**The INSERM 2004 report and its withdrawal.** *Psychothérapie : trois approches évaluées*, Expertise collective, February 2004, Éditions Inserm, XII-553 p (http://hdl.handle.net/10608/146; synthèse http://hdl.handle.net/10608/147; English on NCBI Bookshelf https://ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK7123/). Commissioned by the **DGS** with **UNAFAM** and **FNAP-Psy** under the 2001 *Plan santé mentale*; « environ 1 000 articles »; eleven working sessions May 2002 – December 2003; evidence current to the **second half of 2003**. Experts: Olivier Canceil, Jean Cottraux, Bruno Falissard, Martine Flament, Jacques Miermont, Joel Swendsen, Mardjane Teherani, Jean-Michel Thurin.

Headline conclusions, verbatim from the INSERM press release of 26 February 2004: mild/moderate outpatient depression — « **les thérapies cognitives sont plus efficaces que les traitements antidépresseurs**. Les thérapies psychodynamiques n'ont pas montré une efficacité équivalente à celles des TCC »; inpatient major depression — « les études contrôlées comparant approches psychodynamique et cognitivo-comportementale concluent à la **supériorité de la seconde** »; anxiety disorders — CBT efficacy « la mieux établie » in panic and GAD; schizophrenia acute phase — « **pas d'efficacité des thérapies psychodynamiques** »; borderline personality — **psychodynamic therapies effective at 18 months–4 years**, CBT at 1 year; and « il n'a pas été relevé d'apparition de nouveaux symptômes venant se substituer […] à ceux pris en charge par la thérapie » — **no symptom substitution**.

**What happened to it.** On **5 February 2005** health minister **Philippe Douste-Blazy** announced he had had the report **removed from the ministry website**, telling the *Forum des psys* its « contenu et [sa] promotion vous ont particulièrement offensés ». *Le Monde*, 9 February 2005: « De mémoire de chercheur, c'est la première fois qu'un ministre de la santé conteste une expertise collective menée pendant près de deux ans par un organisme public ». **No counter-expertise was commissioned and INSERM never retracted the report.** The government's later position, from the ministry's answer to a Sénat written question (question published JO Sénat 05/07/2007; **answer published 13/11/2008**, https://www.senat.fr/questions/base/2007/qSEQ070700398.html): the report « a fait l'objet de plusieurs critiques notamment quant à la possibilité de généraliser les résultats aux pratiques réelles de soin », and **la HAS « a accepté de réaliser un travail de synthèse bibliographique des méthodes existantes d'évaluation des psychothérapies »**.

**Its status today (2026-07-28), established with sources:**
1. **It is not, and never was, a clinical guideline** — an evidence synthesis intended « d'aider le commanditaire […] dans sa prise de décision », never adopted by HAS.
2. **It is still publicly available and has never been formally superseded** — ipubli.inserm.fr, HAL (hal-01570642, record last modified 22 September 2025), NCBI Bookshelf. **INSERM has never published a second expertise collective on psychotherapies**; the current catalogue (https://www.inserm.fr/expertise-collective/) contains no successor.
3. **Its evidence cut-off is the second half of 2003 — 23 years old.** Historical context only.
4. **It is explicitly contested in the peer-reviewed French literature.** *L'Évolution psychiatrique* (https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0014385520300761): « Le rapport d'expertise de l'Inserm […] **apparaît aujourd'hui relativement daté, tant au plan méthodologique que dans ses conclusions**… il **ne peut plus être aujourd'hui la référence dominante, en France, pour recommander les « bonnes pratiques » psychothérapiques**. »
5. **French official practice already diverged from it** — HAS 2017 puts *psychothérapie de soutien* first-line for mild EDC and ranks nothing.

> **Both of the usual rhetorical uses are wrong.** It is not current proof that TCC beats psychoanalysis, and its 2005 withdrawal is **not** proof that it was scientifically discredited — the withdrawal was a political act, not a scientific retraction. ⚠ `?` The much-quoted scorecard — « sur 16 troubles […] les TCC […] efficaces de façon établie dans **15** cas, la thérapie familiale dans **cinq** cas et la psychanalyse dans **un** seul cas » — is a **secondary paraphrase**, not INSERM's own wording. **Use the disorder-by-disorder findings, not the scorecard.**

**Autism: from « non consensuelles » (2012) to « non recommandées » (2026).** HAS + ANESM 2012 classed psychoanalytic approaches and packing « **non consensuelles** » — a deliberately diplomatic formulation. The HAS RBP validated **8 January 2026** places « psychanalyse et autres interventions basées sur des approches psychanalytiques » in the explicit **« interventions non recommandées »** list, and **packing** in the harder *"studies having proven their absence of effect"* tier. HAS frames the choice methodologically: « Il appartient à la Haute Autorité de santé de ne recommander que les approches fondées sur les données scientifiques rigoureuses ». **This is the point where the four-decade French psychoanalysis/TCC conflict formally resolved in one clinical domain. It has not resolved in adult psychiatry** — the 2017 depression RBP still lists psychodynamic and analytically-inspired therapies alongside TCC without preference.

**The "paramédicalisation" fight over Mon soutien psy.** The **Syndicat National des Psychologues** published a tribune calling the scheme « un véritable écran de fumée », arguing that the inclusion criteria mean « seules les personnes présentant une "petite anxiété" méritent une consultation psychologique », and that « **La psychologie est une science humaine et doit le rester. Elle n'a pas vocation à être inféodée à la médecine. Les psychologues ne seront jamais des auxiliaires médicaux.** » **Association M3P** maintains a **boycott** and argues the scheme produces a **substitution effect** in public decision-making — citing an ARS refusal to fund free CIDFF 72 psychological consultations for victims of sexual and gender-based violence on the ground that « le dispositif MonSoutienPsy serait suffisant ». **AID-Psy-Co / conventionpsy.fr**, a pro-engagement association, argues the binding constraint is **care-path coherence**: the 12-session cap breaks follow-up for complex trauma, severe burn-out and addictions; conventioned tariffs (€50) sit well below non-conventioned ones (« entre 60 et 120 euros selon les territoires »); and there is no funded coordination time with psychiatry or general practice.

> **Where TCC sits in this fight: nowhere explicitly.** Neither the décrets, the arrêtés, the convention-cadre, nor the inclusion criteria mention any therapeutic modality. A conventioned psychologist may deliver TCC, psychodynamic work, systemic work or anything else. **France reimburses a professional and a session count, not an evidence-based protocol.** The ministry's stated "seconde brique" — « créer une forme de nomenclature d'actes requis pour un certain nombre de pathologies psychiques » — would be the first time French reimbursement attaches to a specified psychotherapeutic technique.

### 7.8 The French dose problem, stated plainly

Three anchors, none reconciled by anything in the record:

| Anchor | Figure | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **HAS 2007 ALD-23** (the only French numeric anchor) | **12–25 sessions of ~45 min** for GAD and panic; **≥25** for OCD; **20–40** for TCC-seule failure | HAS guide médecin, June 2007 |
| **NICE CG113** (panic) | **7–14 therapist hours** total ≈ 7–14 sessions | `[2004]`-tagged, never re-reviewed |
| **English dose–response** | 95% of high-intensity responders improve within **14 sessions** — but only ~39% of cases become responders | Robinson et al. 2020, N = 102,206 |
| **"Mon soutien psy" ceiling** | **12 sessions per calendar year** | R. 162-65 CSS |

**Nothing in the corpus decides what a French patient should expect**, and the Mon soutien psy cap sits **below all three clinical anchors**. AID-Psy-Co cites the WHO framing of 12 sessions as a *minimum* for some psychotrauma care against the cap; the ministry cites the observed 4.8-session average as evidence the cap binds rarely. Both are in the record; neither settles it.

---

## 8. The 2024–2026 frontier

Only developments dated 2024-01-01 → 2026-07-28 count as "current" here.

**Process-based therapy** — operationalised into a testable protocol, first health-system RCT running, **no outcome data published**. See §1.5. The conflict worth naming: the first RCT of PBT is financed by a LOEWE Spitzenprofessur grant **held by PBT's principal proponent** (Hofmann), awarded by the Hessian Ministry of Science and Arts, with Stangier as PI. ⚠ An earlier corpus draft overstated this as "Hofmann is simultaneously funder and co-PI" — he is the grant *holder*.

**Precision psychotherapy** — still protocols and tools, not deployed practice. The flagship treatment-selection effort is at protocol stage (Driessen E. et al., *PLoS ONE* 2025;20(4):e0322124, 2025-04-23, five treatments, two-stage IPD-NMA on Metapsy; **results not published**). **PETRUSHKA**, the closest thing to a live precision tool, is an **antidepressant**-selection tool, not a psychotherapy one (Ostinelli E.G. et al., *Can J Psychiatry* 2025, DOI 10.1177/07067437251322399); **no published trial results were found**. Machine-learning outcome prediction remains fragile — two 2025 systematic reviews report small samples, high overfitting risk and limited generalisability. `?` The Personalized Advantage Index external-validation record is mixed and no 2024–2026 result changed it.

**Single-session interventions — the biggest, cleanest negative-ish result of the period.** The enabling evidence: Schleider J.L. et al., *Annual Review of Clinical Psychology* 2025;21:279–303, DOI 10.1146/annurev-clinpsy-081423-025033 — 24 systematic reviews covering **415 unique trials**; across the 12 meta-analytic reviews **SMD = −0.25, I² = 43.17%**. **The 2026 megastudy that punctured it:** *"A crowdsourced megastudy of 12 digital single-session interventions for depression in US adults"*, **Nature Human Behaviour, 2026-03-02**, DOI 10.1038/s41562-026-02415-6, NCT06856668, **N = 7,505**. Verbatim: *"Nearly all SSIs improved psychological outcomes immediately after completion (d ≤ 0.37). **However, only two SSIs significantly reduced depression at 4-week follow-up (d = 0.14 and 0.15). Unexpectedly, completing an SSI made participants feel less confident and less interested in making changes to overcome depression at 4 weeks, on average (d = 0.05).**"* And in young people: *eClinicalMedicine* 2025, 22 articles / 18 unique digital SSIs, **N = 9,353** — depression at longest follow-up (mean 3.05 months) **g = −0.18 (−0.24 to −0.11), I² = 0%**; evidence for self-injurious thoughts and behaviours *"limited"*/*"inconclusive"*.

**Measurement-based care buys speed, not a different endpoint.** Husain M.I. et al., *JAMA Network Open* 2025;8(9):e2529427, DOI 10.1001/jamanetworkopen.2025.29427 (2025-09-02), N = 154, 7 Pakistani cities, assessor-blinded, 24 weeks: median time to response **2 weeks vs 4**; median time to remission **4 weeks vs 8**; Cox HRs 1.53 (response) and 1.80 (remission). At week 12, response 90.6% vs 67.6%; **by week 24 the between-group differences were no longer significant** (89.1% vs 86.6%, P = .79). Note: the best available MBC-for-depression meta-analysis is still 2021 (Zhu M. et al., *J Clin Psychiatry* 2021;82(5):21r14034 — **7 RCTs, all pharmacotherapy; no study explicitly reported psychotherapy**), so the MBC-in-psychotherapy case rests on the feedback/ROM literature, not on new evidence.

**Combination and sequencing with pharmacotherapy — the most consequential 2026 result.** Čihařová M., Karyotaki E., Harrer M. et al., *Psychotherapy and Psychosomatics*, DOI 10.1159/000550633, e-published **2026-01-21**, searches to 2024-09-01. 75 eligible RCTs; **IPD obtained for only 31 (41.3%), n = 3,703**. Combined > pharmacotherapy alone: post-treatment **SMD 0.45 (0.27–0.64)**, 6 months **0.55 (0.25–0.85)**, 12 months **0.22 (0.02–0.43)**. **No participant-level effect modifiers were found** — gender, age, education, ethnicity, severity, comorbidity, number of previous episodes, episode length, previous treatment and functioning did *not* moderate benefit. ⚠ **Do not read this as anti-combination**; its stated conclusion is the opposite: *"**Combined treatment outperforms pharmacotherapy alone regardless of individual characteristics. Thus, its delivery does not have to be limited to specific patient subgroups.**"* A subgroup signal exists — in trials comparing psychotherapy + **SSRI** vs SSRI alone there was no evidence of a difference (`?` SMD 0.04, −0.08 to 0.17) while the advantage came from non-SSRI antidepressant trials (`?` SMD 0.62, 0.42–0.82) — but the abstract states plainly that *"these findings **were not confirmed in sensitivity analyses**"*. Effect sizes were also **smaller in the trials that shared IPD**. `?` GRADE "low to very low" and "non-significant in two of three publication-bias corrections" are not in the abstract and are unverified.

**Deprescribing: psychotherapy is now part of the recommended taper, on moderate-certainty evidence.** *"Comparison of antidepressant deprescribing strategies in individuals with clinically remitted depression"*, **The Lancet Psychiatry, 2026-01**, searched to 2025-04-06; **76 trials, 17,379 participants** (60 depression, 16 anxiety), mean follow-up 45.9 weeks. Versus abrupt discontinuation for relapse prevention: continuation at standard dose **plus psychological support RR 0.40 (0.26–0.61), NNT 4.3, moderate certainty**; continuation at standard dose RR 0.51 (0.46–0.58), NNT 5.3; **slow tapering plus psychological support RR 0.52 (0.38–0.72), NNT 5.4, moderate certainty**; continuation at reduced dose 0.62 (0.42–0.92), NNT 6.8, low. **Slow tapering alone was NOT better than abrupt discontinuation (RR 0.81, 0.56–1.18, low certainty).** Verbatim: *"In remitted depression, slow tapering plus psychological support is as effective as antidepressant continuation in preventing relapse and superior to abrupt or rapid discontinuation… **Guidelines should promote individualised deprescribing with gradual tapering and structured psychological support.**"* Scope caveat the authors state: for remitted *anxiety* the evidence is limited and *"warrants cautious generalisation"*.

**Psychedelic-assisted therapy: setback, retractions, and a "what exactly is the psychotherapy?" crisis.**
- FDA issued a **Complete Response Letter to Lykos Therapeutics in August 2024** declining MDMA-assisted therapy for PTSD and requesting an additional Phase 3 trial; the FDA made the CRL public on **2025-09-04**.
- **Three MAPS/Lykos papers were retracted by *Psychopharmacology* on 2024-08-10** (DOIs 10.1007/s00213-024-06671-0, -06666-x, -06665-y), retraction reason identical across all three: *"protocol violations amounting to unethical conduct at the MP4 study site… **The authors have subsequently confirmed that they were aware of these violations at the time of submission** of this article, but did not disclose this information… Additionally, the authors also did not fully declare a potential competing interest."* Several authors disagree with the retractions.
- **Psilocybin succeeded where MDMA failed — with a deliberately minimised psychological component.** COMP005 (June 2025): MADRS mean treatment difference **−3.6 [−5.7, −1.5], p < 0.001 at week 6**. COMP006 (2026-02-17): **−3.8 [−5.8, −1.8], p < 0.001**; **39%** of the 25 mg arm achieved ≥25% MADRS reduction. **Part B 26-week results announced 2026-07-07** (~600 patients): benefit maintained through at least week 26, ~30% of week-6 responders remitting after retreatment; **rolling NDA submission and initial FDA review already underway**, final submission Q4 2026, launch anticipated H1 2027 subject to approval **and DEA rescheduling**. **All sponsor-issued topline; no peer-reviewed Phase 3 publication found.** **Effect sizes are small in absolute MADRS terms (−3.6 / −3.8 points)** — which matters for any claim that PAT outperforms CBT.
- **The psychotherapy component is under-reported and strategically renamed.** Seybert C. et al., *The Lancet Psychiatry* 2025;12(1):54–66, DOI 10.1016/S2215-0366(24)00333-X, PROSPERO CRD42022319221, search to 2024-02-04: 45 studies (psilocybin, MDMA, LSD, ayahuasca); interventions *"done heterogeneously"*, completeness *"mostly low"* on an adapted TIDieR checklist — **mean score 5.89 (SD 2.06), range 2–9**; MDMA 7.4 > psilocybin 6.05 > LSD 5.5 > ayahuasca 3.9; therapeutic alliance *"has not been measured as an active component"*. Brusky B., M'Baïlara K., Alla F. & Barrault M., *Journal of Psychopharmacology*, DOI 10.1177/02698811261424204 (2026-03-20), 29 trials / 449 patients against a 4-item common-factors framework: **69% of all trials met all four criteria**; **84% of the 19 psychotherapy-labelled trials** and **40% of the 10 non-psychotherapy-labelled trials** — i.e. the psychological interventions in most psychedelic trials **do qualify as psychotherapy regardless of what they are called**. The naming is argued to be a regulatory strategy: Wolff M., Gukasyan N., Roseman L. & Liknaitzky P., *Drug Science, Policy and Law*, DOI 10.1177/20503245251348272 (2025-06-16) explicitly names the target — *"the emerging trend in psychedelic drug development that views psychotherapy as a strategic liability and seeks to de-emphasize or remove it."* Whether **CBT specifically** should be the default therapy inside PAT is actively contested (*Frontiers in Psychology* 2025;16:1505894, 2025-02-05, arguing against defaulting to CBT). `?` A practitioner survey found *"there is no consensus on certain psychological support strategies"* — but **only 40 qualified respondents**, non-probability sampling, data collected March–July 2023 (Bender D.A. et al., *J Clin Psychiatry* 2025;86(1):24m15521, 2025-02-05). ⚠ A claim that "Compass Pathways-trained respondents were comparatively neuromodulatory" **does not exist in that paper** and has been deleted; the contrast it draws is MAPS/CIIS-trained vs all others.

**Ketamine + psychotherapy: the additive hypothesis took a hit.** Moore T.M. et al., *Journal of Affective Disorders* 2025;381:233–241, DOI 10.1016/j.jad.2025.04.041: *"**Combining psychotherapy with ketamine treatment did not significantly enhance symptom improvement compared to ketamine alone**"*; moderation — younger females did better with combined, older males better with ketamine alone. Caveat: naturalistic help-seeking sample, not an RCT. Separately, **FDA approved esketamine (Spravato) as monotherapy** for treatment-resistant depression on **2025-01-21**, removing the required oral antidepressant — while **NICE TA854 still does not recommend esketamine**, and its **June 2024** review found the evidence insufficient to change that. **The two regulators are in opposite positions on the same drug.**
