## 10. Where this dossier's own sources disagree

These are live contradictions inside the research corpus. Where one side is clearly more recent or more authoritative, that is said and why. Where neither is, both are given.

1. **NICE NG222 rec 1.5.3 — RESOLVED.** One corpus file quoted the original June-2022 wording (*"unless that is the person's preference"*) as current; two others documented the **October 2025 amendment** to *"Only offer it if that is the person's informed preference."* **The amendment record on NICE's own Update-information page settles it** — the live text is the October 2025 wording, and the `[2022]` tag conceals the edit. The stale quotation was corrected in the since-deleted `raw/gap-2.md`, and the 2022 wording was deliberately kept in `raw/guidelines.md` as the "before" half of a documented before/after pair. Both files are recoverable from the `me` repository at `15e599a` (see [§12](#12-provenance)); only the resolution above survives in this dossier.

2. **NHS Talking Therapies 2024-25 recovery rate for IPT — UNRESOLVED.** One file gives **IPT 44.8% recovery / 62.1% improvement**; another gives *"Interpersonal Psychotherapy had the highest recovery (**48.7%**) and improvement (**62.1%**)"*. **Both cite the same NHS England Digital therapy-based-outcomes chapter for 2024-25.** The improvement figure matches; **the recovery figure differs by 3.9 points and one of the two is wrong.** The chapter is published as **experimental statistics**, and in either case the comparison with CBT (41.5% recovery / 60.7% improvement) is **unadjusted, non-randomised and confounded by case mix, step and severity** — it is descriptive, not a comparative-effectiveness finding.

3. **Mon soutien psy session cap — RESOLVED in favour of the primary sources.** Two files state a flat 12-session cap *"still in force, unchanged"* and *"re-verified against ameli.fr 2026-07-28"*. A third documents **LOI n° 2026-492 du 12 juin 2026, art. 13**, adding L. 162-58 III: **no cap for minors in ALD within a protocole de soins**, read directly on Légifrance and confirmed in plain language by service-public.gouv.fr on 16 June 2026. **The statutory text wins. "Unchanged" is false.**

4. **Mon soutien psy tiers payant from 1 October 2026 — RESOLVED in favour of the primary sources, with a caveat.** One file treats it as established, sourced to **service-public.gouv.fr F34917 (vérifié 3 juillet 2026)**, **LOI n° 2025-1403 du 30 décembre 2025** on Légifrance, and the L. 162-58 version banner *"en vigueur … au 1er octobre 2026"*. Another says *"No Légifrance, ameli or service-public text confirming this was fetched… Treat as unverified."* **The second is a statement about that researcher's retrieval, not about the evidence.** Three primary artefacts support the claim. **The genuine residual uncertainty is narrower:** the **application décret is still pending**, and `?` **the LFSS 2026 article number carrying the measure could not be verified in the JO text**.

5. **Mon soutien psy remote delivery — RESOLVED in favour of the arrêté.** Two files describe follow-up sessions as simply *"which may be delivered by vidéotransmission"*, with no cap and no bilan requirement. Two others record the **arrêté du 17 juin 2026** imposing a **20% annual cap on remote conventioned activity at practitioner level**, a **ban on exclusively remote practice**, and **mandatory in-person entretien AND bilan** — quoted verbatim and verified character-for-character against Légifrance on 2026-07-28. **The arrêté wins.** Caveat: it binds each psychologist from their own signature date and is a one-year instrument.

6. **Number of participating psychologists — RECONCILED as a time series, with one figure rejected.** One file states *"~5,500 partner psychologists as of early April 2025"* sourced to info.gouv.fr; another rejects *"6,200+"* as appearing only in secondary press/aggregator pages. **These are not in conflict: they are different numbers from different dates with different sourcing.** The defensible series is **5 217 (28 Feb 2025, Sénat n° 787) → ~5 500 (early April 2025, info.gouv.fr) → 7 799 (May 2026, DMSMP)**, with a professional association reporting « environ 8 000 » in April 2026. **"6,200+" remains unsourced and should not be used.**

7. **WHO's position on psychological therapy for PTSD — RESOLVED; one file is a decade stale.** One file's guideline map lists WHO's entry as *"Guidelines for conditions specifically related to stress, 6 Aug 2013 | 13 years old"*, with no mention of mhGAP. Another documents the **mhGAP guideline 3rd edition, published 20 November 2023**, ISBN 978-92-4-008427-8, explicitly superseding the 2010 and 2015 documents, with **STR1** (adults; individual and group face-to-face trauma-focused CBT, digital/remote TF-CBT, EMDR, stress management; **CONDITIONAL, moderate certainty**) and **STR2** (children/adolescents; **STRONG, moderate certainty**). **The 2023 guideline is WHO's current word.** The 2013 entry presents a superseded document as WHO's position on exactly the question (EMDR vs TF-CBT) that same file is arguing about.

8. **HAS 2007 ALD 23 guide médecin — RESOLVED in favour of the file that read it.** One file says *"I could not open the HAS 2007 document itself"* and treats claims about it as unsourced. Another **quotes it verbatim at length** from https://www.has-sante.fr/upload/docs/application/pdf/guide_medecin_troubles_anxieux.pdf, with its verification log recording that *"every French quotation [was] re-read from the HAS PDF and confirmed verbatim"* (12–25 séances for GAD and panic, ≥25 for TOC, 20–40 for TCC-seule failure, *"Le contact avec le thérapeute reste indispensable"*). **The document is accessible and those quotations are verified.** ⚠ **But the specific claim that it names "TCC and EMDR as treatments of choice" for psychotrauma is a different claim, and it remains unsourced** — it rests only on an EMDR advocacy page and a 2023 French thesis. The verified quotations concern **GAD, panic and OCD**, not PTSD.

9. **Status of relaxation — GENUINELY OPEN, and one file overstates it.** One file's Superseded table states flatly *"Relaxation as a routine CBT package component (depression, panic) — SUPERSEDED — evidence of harm"*. Another carries an explicit 2026-07-28 correction saying the opposite: *"do not state relaxation has been demoted by guideline bodies. AASM 2021 still conditionally recommends relaxation therapy as a single-component therapy… Report it as an open contradiction, not a superseded practice."* **Verified 2026-07-28: AASM's 2021 behavioural-psychological CPG does conditionally recommend relaxation therapy, and the April 2026 combination CPG did not revisit it.** A third file adds that **applied relaxation is a live NICE recommendation for GAD (CG113 rec 1.2.18)**. **Correct statement: relaxation is a negative component in specific packages (internet CBT for depression; panic CBT; insomnia component NMA where its CI touches 1.00) and simultaneously a live conditional guideline recommendation for insomnia and a live co-equal option for GAD. It is contested, not superseded.**

10. **Status of the PBT trial NCT06517589 — THREE INCONSISTENT ACCOUNTS, TWO IN THE SAME FILE.** (a) A verification log states the registry *"still reads Status: Recruiting, start 2024-01-29, estimated completion 2025-07-30"* and **explicitly removes an earlier claim about a September 2025 recruitment close**. (b) The **same file's** Unresolved section still asserts the trial *"completed recruitment 2025-09-30"* and that PBACE *"runs to 2029"* — **both facts the same file retracted elsewhere.** (c) A third file says it was *"registered 24 July 2024"*. **Partial reconciliation:** (c) is compatible with (a) — ClinicalTrials.gov *registration/first-posted* date and *study start* date are different fields, so "registered 24 July 2024, study start 29 January 2024" is internally consistent. **The genuine error is the surviving recruitment-close and PBACE-to-2029 claims in the Unresolved section, which the verification log had already retracted.** Use the verification-log version: **Recruiting, no results posted, no completion, no results publication found at 2026-07-28.**

11. **Status of the 2004 INSERM expertise collective — RESOLVED.** One file establishes with sources that it *"is still publicly available and has never been formally superseded"*, that INSERM has never published a successor, and that the 2005 ministerial withdrawal was **political rather than scientific**. Another refers to it in passing as *"(almost certainly superseded, and politically contested at the time — needs its own check)"*. **The first file did the check; the parenthetical assumption is wrong on the "superseded" half.**

12. **Authorship of the transdiagnostic meta-analysis — UNRESOLVED.** One file attributes *"Transdiagnostic psychological interventions for emotional disorders: A comprehensive meta-analysis"*, *J Affect Disord* 2025, DOI 10.1016/j.jad.2025.119537, to **"Jiménez-Orenga N, et al."**; another attributes the same paper — same journal, same volume 388:119537, same 94 trials / 108 comparisons / 12,443 patients / g = 0.59 / I² 78.9 — to **"Díaz-García A, et al."** **Identical study, two different first authors. Cite by DOI and title until this is checked.**

13. **Whether the guided/unguided distinction is the right frame at all — RESOLVED against both older framings.** One file makes the guided-vs-unguided binary its organising claim; another downgrades unguided self-help for OCD and then immediately quotes the same authors saying it *"holds important potential"*. **The 2026 BJPsych support-levels NMA (DOI 10.1192/bjp.2026.10653) resolves this against both**: it abandons the binary and finds no-support formats comparable to therapist-guided ones when pre-intervention human contact exists. Note that at the time the corpus was assembled, **no file other than the dedicated gap file cited it**.

14. **NICE HTE8 → HTG675 migration date — RESOLVED.** One file dates the HTE8/HTE9/MTG70 renumbering vaguely as *"2025–2026"*; another gives HTG624 a *"last reviewed 20 June 2022"* (a month after MTG70's 20 May 2022 publication, which is itself odd); a third gives the specific and verifiable *"HTE8 migrated to HTG675 in December 2025"*. **Verified 2026-07-28 on the NICE Update-information page: December 2025.** Use that.

15. **"Group CBT — DOWNGRADED" as a general heading — OVERSTATED.** The downgrade rests on one OCD network meta-analysis and one PTSD guideline rationale; the same corpus section contains the counter-evidence (NG222's group CBT and group BA as first-line options for less severe depression, **ranked above individual CBT** on cost-effectiveness) but keeps the generalising heading. **Scope the claim to OCD, PTSD and social anxiety; it is false for depression.**

16. **The dodo-bird claim's flatness — see §5.6.** *"Non-directive supportive counselling is the only therapy CBT reliably beats — everything else is a tie"* and *"there is currently no reason to choose one therapy over the other"* sit against the corpus's own within-CBT differentiation. Both can be true (brands converge while components diverge) but the equivalence claim is stated at a flatness the rest of the corpus does not support.

17. **"Children and adolescents" as a population where CBT works less well — see §3.16.** The supporting figures are all from **depression** trials, while paediatric anxiety CBT shows OR 5.45 vs waitlist, NNTB 3, moderate-quality Cochrane evidence. **Read as "CBT underperforms in youth *depression*".**

18. **VR exposure — the corpus contained the answer to its own open question.** One file leaves it open (*"treat the panic-specific negative component finding as domain-limited (? — I did not fetch the 2024–2025 VR meta-analyses)"*) while another already carries the resolution: **Chang et al., *J Glob Health* 2026;16:04090**, 26 RCTs, 1,649 participants, restricted to clinically diagnosed samples, plus the German S3 positioning. **Resolved in §8.**

19. **Mon soutien psy uptake figures — ACKNOWLEDGED AND UNRESOLVED, correctly.** Two French ministry documents released the same week in June 2026 give *"plus de 1 M patients"* vs *"près de 1,8 million"*, and 461 756 vs 519 051 beneficiaries for 2025. The corpus flags this correctly (*"Do not present either as the figure"*). **Any downstream synthesis must not pick one.**

20. **Session-dose anchors — CONFLICTING, WITH NO RESOLUTION ANYWHERE.** See §7.8. HAS 2007 prescribes ≥25 sessions for OCD and 12–25 for GAD/panic; NICE CG113 frames panic in therapist *hours* (7–14) and CG31 frames OCD in therapist hours (10 h threshold); English dose–response data give 95% of high-intensity responders improving within 14 sessions (on a ~39% response base); and the Mon soutien psy cap of 12 sits **below all three**. **Nothing in the record decides what a French patient should expect.**

21. **ISTSS recency arithmetic — MINOR BUT WORTH FIXING.** One file says twice that *"the current version is 7 years old"*. The documents date from **November 2018** (amended **19 March 2019**) against a dossier date of **28 July 2026** — that is **seven years from the amendment and over eight from the 31 March 2018 RCT search cut-off**. State which anchor you mean.

22. **NICE NG116 "last reviewed" date — `?` AN UNRESOLVED FETCH DISCREPANCY.** On 2026-07-28, two live fetches returned different values from different URL casings: `https://www.nice.org.uk/guidance/ng116` → *"Last reviewed: 8 April 2025 — We found no new evidence that affects the recommendations in this guideline"*; `https://www.nice.org.uk/Guidance/Ng116` → *"Last reviewed: 23 July 2026 — We reviewed this guideline and will not be updating the recommendations on adapted trauma-focused treatment."* One corpus file closed the 2026 date as unsupported; another re-opened it. **This dossier states 8 April 2025.** Either way **NG116 rec 1.6.5 stands**; only the review date is in question. A human should open `https://www.nice.org.uk/guidance/ng116/history`.

---

## 11. Known gaps and unresolved questions

Two kinds of item are listed here and they are different. **§11.1** are gaps that were *identified and deliberately not researched* — they are holes in this dossier's coverage, not in the literature. **§11.2 onwards** are the "Unresolved" items carried forward from each raw research file: things that were attempted and could not be sourced to an acceptable standard.

**125 items in two independent number series — cite them unambiguously.** §11.1 runs **1–20** and is its own series; §11.2 through §11.10 share a single series running **1–105**. The two overlap, so a bare "item 14" is ambiguous. Write **"§11.1 item 14"** for a coverage gap and **"§11 item 14"** for an unresolved item.

### 11.1 Identified but NOT researched — the honest list

**Content gaps that would change what a reader does:**

1. **Therapist competence, treatment fidelity, drift and supervision — is the TCC actually delivered the TCC that was tested?** The corpus establishes that therapists differ by up to 2× in recovery rates (§5.7) and that guidelines mandate manual-based delivery (§2.8), but it contains **nothing** on how competence is measured, whether measured competence predicts outcome, or how far routine delivery departs from protocol. `CTS-R` returns **0 hits** across 8,385 lines of corpus; `fidelity` 1; `drift` 2 (both incidental). France coverage stops at who may legally practise and AFTCC's certification hours — it never asks whether any of that predicts outcome. **This is the single most decision-relevant missing block**: it is the bridge between the efficacy literature and the therapist a patient would actually sit in front of, and **exposure under-use — the best-documented drift phenomenon in the field — does not appear at all.**
2. **Economics and cost-effectiveness — the criterion guidelines actually optimise.** NICE's ordering of options is explicitly cost-effectiveness-driven (the ranking *guided self-help → group CBT → group BA → individual CBT*, and NG116's rejection of group TF-CBT as *"not clinically or cost effective"*), yet the corpus gives **not a single ICER, cost per QALY, cost per session, or economic model input**. `QALY` = 0 hits; `econom` = 3 incidental hits. Without this the dossier cannot explain **why the recommended thing is often the cheap thing rather than the best thing**, and cannot answer the practical French question: 12 séances at €50 reimbursed 60%, vs €60–120/session libéral, vs a €660 M national programme whose value is contested in the Sénat report quoted in §7.6.
3. **Cultural adaptation and evidence outside high-income Western settings, including task-shifted / non-specialist delivery.** The corpus notes that country of origin swings effect sizes by up to **0.61 SD — larger than the whole treatment effect in low-risk-of-bias trials** — and dismisses it in one line as *"almost certainly methodological"*. **That is the corpus's own largest unexplained moderator and it receives no follow-up.** WHO's CBT-derived scalable manuals are catalogued (**PM+**, 2018, *"Aspects of Cognitive Behavioural Therapy (CBT) have been changed to make them feasible in communities that do not have many specialists"*, 5 × 90-min sessions; **PM+ individual training manual**, 19 November 2025; **Group PM+ training manual**, 2026; **SH+**, 2021, *"based on acceptance and commitment therapy (ACT), a form of cognitive behavioural therapy (CBT)"*; **Step-by-Step** and *Doing What Matters in Times of Stress*, June 2026, of which WHO says *"both of which have demonstrated effectiveness in multiple randomized controlled trials"*) — **but with zero outcome data reported for any of them.** No file addresses whether culturally adapted CBT outperforms unadapted CBT, or how TCC performs with migrant and racialised populations in France. `LMIC`, `non-Western`, `task-shar`, `ethnic`, `minorit` all return **0 hits**.
4. **TCC in older adults.** Children and adolescents are well covered; the other tail is essentially absent. `older adult` returns 4 hits corpus-wide, of which **only one is substantive** (the Cochrane review on anxiety in older adults, §4.1); `late-life`, `geriatr` and `personnes âgées` return **0** — the HAS 2025–2030 programme theme *"personnes âgées"* is listed and never followed up, and Germany's NVL explicitly defers its *"Ältere Patient*innen"* chapter. **A dossier claiming completeness cannot be silent on the population where comorbid physical illness, polypharmacy, sensory and cognitive impairment most change how TCC is delivered — and where the pharmacological alternative is most hazardous.**
5. **TCC in physical illness — and the ME/CFS (PACE) controversy, omitted entirely.** Chronic pain (§3.12), insomnia (§3.8) and health anxiety (§3.13) are covered, but CBT in medical illness more broadly is absent: `cancer`, `diabet`, `IBS`, `chronic fatigue`, `myalgic`, `functional somatic`, `long-term condition`, `perinatal` all return ~0 hits, and NICE CG91 (depression with a chronic physical health problem, 2009) is left as an unresolved status item. **More seriously for a dossier whose remit includes controversies: the PACE trial and the ME/CFS reversal do not appear anywhere.** That is the highest-profile public controversy about CBT of the last fifteen years, it produced a guideline-level downgrade (**NICE NG206, 2021**), and its absence is conspicuous next to 114 KB on decline effects and publication bias.
6. **What the patient actually does and experiences: homework, alliance, preference, and service-user-reported negative experiences.** **Homework — the defining behavioural demand of TCC and its best-studied within-treatment process variable — appears once in 8,385 lines** (`homework` = 1 hit; `devoir` = 0). Therapeutic alliance in CBT gets 6 incidental mentions. Harms are covered thoroughly but almost entirely from **trial-reporting** data; the only patient-report source is a single 224-person survey inside a *World Psychiatry* commentary, and the corpus itself flags the ~10× discrepancy between that survey and trial-derived harm rates as unresolved (§5.9). `patient perspective`, `service user`, `bibliother` all return **0**. **The dossier can say how well TCC works but not what doing it feels like, what patients refuse, or why they drop out — which is what determines whether a given person would complete a course.**

**Currency and sourcing gaps in what *is* covered:**

7. **The WHO gap in one corpus file** — see [§10 item 7](#10-where-this-dossiers-own-sources-disagree). One file presents a **2013** WHO document as WHO's current word on exactly the question it is arguing about, while mhGAP 3rd edition (20 November 2023) is documented at length elsewhere in the same corpus.
8. **The Mon soutien psy "unchanged" claims** — see [§10 items 3 and 5](#10-where-this-dossiers-own-sources-disagree). Two files stamped *"recency checked / re-verified 2026-07-28, still the current rule, unchanged"* miss **two** changes: the LOI n° 2026-492 deplafonnement for minors in ALD, and the arrêté du 17 juin 2026's **20% remote-activity ceiling**. **"Unchanged" is false on both counts, and the 20% cap is precisely the kind of rule a reader would act on.**
9. **A probably-stale procedural claim about Mon soutien psy that no primary source supports.** One file states *"After 12 sessions without improvement, a concertation with the psychologist and a psychiatrist is required before a further 12-session cycle."* **This looks like a survival of the 2022 MonPsy launch design.** The current rule, systematically audited, is a hard cap with a single statutory exception (§7.3); ameli warns that money can be reclaimed for exceeding it; and the **arrêté du 13 mai 2025 rewrote the psychiatrist-opinion clause into a dérogation about antidepressant/hypnotic treatment duration, not a renewal mechanism**. **If that sentence is stale it is materially misleading about how many sessions a patient can actually get.**
10. **The app-engagement benchmark is eight-year-old, pre-LLM panel data.** Baumel et al. 2019 (median 30-day retention **3.3%**) covers **August 2017 – July 2018** and is used in a 2026 dossier as the anchor for how apps behave outside trials. The corpus concedes **no post-2020 independent replication was found**, and that everything sourced for 2024–2026 is either trial dropout (far better than in-the-wild retention) or vendor-reported. **The number is doing heavy interpretive work — it is the main counterweight to the app efficacy literature — and it should not stand unreplaced.**
11. **The NICE digital-therapy product lists are a supply-side snapshot presented as a clinical recommendation set.** HTG675 is *"last reviewed 14 January 2025"* and its own text states *"This guidance will be reviewed within 4 years and the recommendations may change"* — published 16 May 2023, so **review due by May 2027**. **Two of the three originally recommended depression products (Beating the Blues, Deprexis) have already been removed for commercial withdrawal, not efficacy.** No corpus file states the review deadline, and **none checks whether SilverCloud/Amwell, Koa Health, Iona Mind, Wysa or Cerina are still trading.**
12. **US prescription digital therapeutics are described in their 2024 clearance-and-launch form.** Every verified fact about Rejoyn is launch-era (FDA clearance March 2024; US launch 14 August 2024 at $200/$50; GB launch 17 June 2025). The corpus **openly concedes it could not verify whether Rejoyn is still actively marketed in mid-2026**. Given that the category's own headline finding is commercial failure (Pear Therapeutics Chapter 11; Woebot shut down; Ash withdrawn from the UK), **an unverified "still on the market" assumption for three products is the weakest link in that section. Big Health's corporate status is not checked at all.**
13. **The two cross-disorder anchors are calibrated on searches 2.5 and 3.5 years old, with no note that Metapsy is a living database.** Cuijpers *JAMA Psychiatry* 2025 searches to 2024-01-01; Cuijpers *World Psychiatry* 2024 searches to 2023-01-01. **Every disorder-level claim in the largest file in the corpus hangs off those pooled estimates**, and the same group publishes continuously — other files already cite 2025–2026 outputs from it (Čihařová IPD-MA searched to 2024-09-01; Cuijpers *World Psychiatry* 2026, DOI 10.1002/wps.70065). **A refreshed unified series or an updated absolute-response paper would move the anchors and therefore everything hanging off them.**
14. **The APA depression-guideline citation trap** — see [§9 D](#9-superseded-abandoned-or-contested--do-not-rely-on-this). The landing page now issues its suggested citation as *"American Psychological Association. (2025)"* for the February-2019 document. **Nobody in the corpus records it.**
15. **Whether the HAS 2007 ALD n°23 guide médecin is still served by HAS as a live document.** The corpus correctly labels it historical, but does not connect two facts it holds separately: the **January 2025 revision of the ALD n°23 actes-et-prestations recorded "Suppression de la psychothérapie" and "Suppression du psychologue"**. **Whether the 2007 guide médecin itself has been withdrawn or archived alongside that scope change is unestablished — and it matters, because it is the only French document specifying TCC content or dose at all.**
16. **There is no verified European training standard.** The only document EABCT links is a PDF titled **"Training-and-Accreditation-2002-2013"** — *the filename is itself the deprecation signal*. The corpus honestly refuses to quote numbers, **but the dossier therefore has no verified European standard at all, and a reader could reasonably assume the 2013 document is current because nothing says otherwise.**
17. **Germany's DiGA figures are a single-day snapshot from a review article, not from BfArM.** The corpus instructs the reader not to quote them in 2026 without re-reading the BfArM DiGA-Verzeichnis — **but then leaves them as the only numbers in the section, so they will be quoted.** The delisting count (six for failing to show a positive healthcare effect) is that section's rhetorical payload.
18. **Cochrane currency was not formally checked.** CD003388.pub4 (PTSD, 2013-12-13), CD008704.pub2 (third-wave vs other therapies, 2013) and CD008705 (2010) are cited as the Cochrane position. The corpus flags all three as historical and establishes CD008704 is un-updated and un-withdrawn. **What it does not check is whether Cochrane has since flagged any of them as "no longer current" under its own currency policy, or whether a replacement protocol is registered** — which is the difference between *"old but standing"* and *"formally stale"*. **CD003388 in particular is described as "the base layer under several current guidelines."**
19. **The VA/DoD "Update of CPG in Progress" marker was not mapped.** MDD v4.0 (2022, evidence to January 2021) and PTSD/ASD v4.0 (2023, evidence to 4 May 2022) are cited as current while the CPG index page flags **some** guidelines as under update **without establishing which**. Two of the corpus's most heavily used guideline sources rest on evidence reviewed five and four years ago, and the PTSD CPG is doing decisive work — including the **EMDR Strong-for grading that another corpus file flags as contested**.
20. **Germany's expired S3 anxiety guideline is quoted as in force in the same file that records its expiry** — see [§8](#8-the-20242026-frontier). A guideline past its *Gültigkeitsdatum* with an unregistered revision **is not "in force"**, and its VR recommendations are the only guideline-level VR positioning in the corpus.

### 11.2 Unresolved from the foundations / theory research

1. **A French institutional anchor for the definition of TCC.** No primary HAS document defining TCC or listing its recommendations could be reached. Every French-language result returned was a therapist directory, a teleconsultation marketing page or a private-practice site — all asserting HAS endorsement and quoting remission figures ("60 à 80 %") **without citing any HAS document**, and all rejected. **Where to look next:** has-sante.fr recommendation search for "psychothérapie" / "troubles anxieux" / "épisode dépressif caractérisé"; AFTCC's own site for its statutory definition; the Mon soutien psy documentation on ameli.fr for the state's operative definition.
2. **APA Division 12 and WHO official *definitions* of CBT** — not searched. ABCT and NICE were used instead.
3. **Cuijpers et al. 2019 component-studies full text** — Taylor & Francis returned HTTP 403. **Where to look next:** the Vrije Universiteit repository copy.
4. **The renewal meta-analysis's quantitative results** (Wang et al. 2024, *Neurosci Biobehav Rev* 159:105606, PMID 38431150) — ScienceDirect returned HTTP 403; design-level findings come from the abstract, pooled effect sizes were not obtained. ⚠ **Correction worth carrying:** this meta-analysis supports **multiple contexts** but its finding on additional cues is that they can act as **safety signals and "protection from extinction" cues** — which cuts **against**, not for, uncritical use of retrieval cues.
5. **Behavioural activation's neurobiological mechanism.** Claims about dorsal ACC activation and default-mode-network regulation surfaced only in search summaries with no traceable primary citation of acceptable quality. **Not asserted anywhere in this dossier.**
6. **Whether PBT outperforms CBT** — structurally unresolvable until at least 2026/27. **Any statement about PBT's clinical superiority must be marked as pending.**
7. **Aaron Beck's death and its effect on the model's stewardship** — widely reported as 1 November 2021 but not sourced from an acceptable primary channel; omitted rather than asserted.
8. **A high-level 2025–2026 review of "the state of CBT theory"** — none located. The closest current syntheses are all sub-domain-specific.

### 11.3 Unresolved from the techniques / protocols research

9. **Unified Protocol long-term follow-ups.** Two papers exist by title (a 12-month follow-up in *Behavior Therapy*; a three-year follow-up in *J Mood Anxiety Disord* 2023) but only titles were seen. **No findings from them are asserted here.**
10. **The 2023 UP RCT-only meta-analysis** (*Clinical Psychology: Science and Practice* 2023;30(2):208–221) — abstract seen via a search-result highlight on a paywalled page; its reported finding is **not asserted as verified**.
11. **Robinson, Delgadillo & Kellett (2020)'s "4–26 sessions" range** — read from a search summary, not the article. The companion *Depression and Anxiety* dose–response figures (7 / 14 sessions) **were** verified.
12. **A formal deprecation of "thought stopping" by a guideline or professional body** — none located. **Where to look next:** BABCP position statements; IOCDF professional guidance; Abramowitz, Deacon & Whiteside, *Exposure Therapy for Anxiety: Principles and Practice*, 2nd ed., Guilford, 2019.
13. **Catharsis / abreaction as a named standalone technique** — sourceable only indirectly, through the Cochrane debriefing review's inclusion criterion. **No current guideline names "abreaction" or "catharsis" to reject it.**
14. **NICE NG222 Tables 1 and 2 in NICE's own wording** — nice.org.uk returns HTTP 403 to one fetcher; the session-count figures come from the NICE-authored BMJ guideline summary in author-proof form, where the tables are partly garbled by PDF extraction. (A second corpus file did read both tables in full and confirmed the ordering claim.) **Where to look next:** the guideline PDF, or the NCBI Bookshelf mirror.
15. **NHS Talking Therapies Manual Tables 2 and 7** and the operational definition of a completed course — the PDF resisted extraction again on 2026-07-28. **Partly resolved:** no version 8 exists; v7 (March 2024) is current. `?` The national standard counts *"a course of treatment (two or more clinical sessions)"* — but that phrase comes from an NHS England summary deck, not the manual body.
16. **AFTCC and EABCT positions** on inhibitory learning, safety behaviours, debriefing or thought stopping — not searched.
17. **Whether the Craske 2022 "OptEx Nexus" worksheets are freely available** — the supplemental material is deposited at CaltechAUTHORS (https://authors.library.caltech.edu/records/npp85-fp440, `1-s2.0-S0005796722000407-mmc1.docx`, 9.6 MB) and reportedly contains the associative-network, exposure-planning and exposure-therapy worksheets plus case vignettes; **not downloaded or verified**.
18. **The Wang et al. 2026 OCD corrigendum** (*Br J Psychiatry* 228(3):292, PMID 41431266) carries no abstract and its content could not be retrieved — **which figures it corrects is unknown**.

### 11.4 Unresolved from the disorder-by-disorder research

19. **Cuijpers 2025 Table 2 and the GRADE table** — the per-disorder breakdown by control type, the per-disorder publication-bias-adjusted and low-RoB-only estimates, and the GRADE ratings were not obtained (403s and truncated extractions). **Where to look next:** the JAMA article page or the Radboud PDF, plus Supplement 1 eAppendices 2–4.
20. **Cuijpers 2024 Results section** — control-group response rates, pooled RRs, NNTs and sensitivity analyses not obtained. **Where to look next:** the Wiley Free Access article and the OSF supplement.
21. **Guidelines for substance use disorders** — no NICE / HAS / VA-DoD / ASAM guideline on psychosocial treatment of SUD was retrieved. **No first-line-status claim for CBT in SUD is made here** beyond the Society of Clinical Psychology "strong recommendation", which is a research-appraisal verdict, not a clinical guideline.
22. **Guidelines for health anxiety** — none found; health anxiety is not covered by CG113 or CG159.
23. **CBT-E-specific outcome figures.** The frequently quoted CBT-E vs IPT remission rates (65.5% vs 33.3% post-treatment; 69.4% vs 49.0% at follow-up) came only from a search-engine summary and **are not asserted here**. Also unretrieved: the per-outcome Hedges' g values from Bruns et al. 2025.
24. **A post-2016 network meta-analysis for adult OCD** — none found. **The adult OCD evidence base as currently synthesised is a decade old and its NICE guideline two decades old.**
25. **French guidance beyond depression** — no HAS RBP located for anxiety disorders, OCD, PTSD, insomnia/CBT-I, eating disorders or ADHD in adults.
26. **Adverse effects / deterioration rates** — almost every source says harms were not systematically recorded. **No meta-analysis of CBT-attributable deterioration was found. This is a genuine hole in the literature, not a retrieval failure.**
27. **Two unlocatable citations, flagged and excluded from reliance:** a *Behaviour Research and Therapy* 2022 single-vs-multi-session exposure meta-analysis (no author list, volume or DOI; ScienceDirect 403), and a *General Hospital Psychiatry* 2024 internet health-anxiety meta-analysis (**no authors, volume, DOI or URL — not locatable at all**).
28. **Whether the NICE OCD update extends coverage to children under 8** — not stated on the project page.
29. **NICE NG87's exact recommendation wording** for non-pharmacological treatment in adults — still not retrieved (currency verified, wording not).
30. **Papola et al. 2023 format-level NMA for panic** (*Psychological Medicine*, PMID 37132646) and **de Ponti et al. 2024** (*J Anxiety Disord* 104:102881) — not retrieved.

### 11.5 Unresolved from the third-wave research

31. **Whether HAS ever published its psychotrauma recommendation** — re-searched 2026-07-28, **still nothing**: only the 2020 note de cadrage, its mirrors, and 2020 press coverage announcing recommendations "prévues en 2021". **Working conclusion: as of 2026-07-28 France still has no published HAS clinical practice recommendation on psychotrauma. State this as "no published recommendation found after a targeted search", never as "HAS recommends X".**
32. **Whether HAS / AFTCC / INSERM have taken any position on third-wave therapies specifically** (ACT, MBCT, DBT, schema therapy) as opposed to TCC generically.
33. **VA/DoD 2023 recommendation-by-recommendation strengths** — the full CPG PDF failed on TLS certificate validation. Recommendations 4, 10, 12, 13, 26 and 27 are quoted via the **ECRI Guidelines Trust profile** (ID 22), cross-checked against the VA National Center for PTSD clinician's guide and the VA landing page. **This still blocks the Strong/Weak EMDR conflict below.**
34. **`?` Whether VA/DoD 2023 grades EMDR "Strong" or "Weak".** The *Annals of Internal Medicine* synopsis lists EMDR inside the strong psychotherapy recommendation; a 2025 comparison table in the *Journal of EMDR Practice and Research* lists VA/DoD 2023 EMDR as "Weak"; EMDRIA describes it as "Strong For". **Not resolvable without the CPG's own recommendation table.**
35. **The exact citation of the "more recent high quality trial (n = 155)"** in which MCT did not beat CBT, cited as reference 43 in Heinitz/Schefft 2023.
36. **Whether CFT yielded zero eligible trials in the 2023 depression NMA** — the paper names WBT, FAP and IBCT as yielding none and does not list CFT among the eight analysed treatments, but does not state CFT's fate explicitly.
37. **Full text of the depression-relapse-prevention NMA** (*Transl Psychiatry*, 2023-09-28) — PMC served a reCAPTCHA; per-timepoint results come from an automated page read and the author list is unverified.
38. **DBT for PTSD (DBT-PTSD)** — a 2024 systematic review exists in *Eur J Psychotraumatol* but was not read; DBT-PTSD's standing relative to TF-CBT is therefore not covered.
39. **German S3 guidelines** (DGPPN personality disorders; DeGPT psychotrauma) — not searched. They are the main continental European counterweight to NICE for DBT/ST/MBT.
40. **Adverse effects of ACT, DBT, schema therapy and MCT** — harms data exist for mindfulness but **no equivalent systematic harms literature was found for the other third-wave therapies**. Gloster et al. 2020 state that for ACT *"no counterindications or iatrogenic effects have been reported to our knowledge, **though they have not been extensively studied in an explicit manner**"*. **Absence of evidence, not evidence of absence.**

### 11.6 Unresolved from the efficacy / controversies research

41. **Relapse/recurrence rates after CBT for depression at defined follow-ups** — search summaries repeatedly quoted "relapse 25–31% after CBT vs 50–78% in controls" but attributed it to no identifiable primary source.
42. **Furukawa 2014 "waiting list may be a nocebo" primary figures** — paywalled; the direction of the finding is corroborated but the numbers are `?`.
43. **Cuijpers et al. 2010 *BJP* publication-bias trim-and-fill figures** — the 2010 paper itself remains unread; Driessen 2015 transcribes them exactly, so the second-hand transcription is sound.
44. **de Vries Y.A. et al., "The cumulative effect of reporting and citation biases on the apparent efficacy of treatments: the case of depression", *Psychological Medicine* (2018)** — surfaced but not read. **This is likely the single strongest "how much is the literature inflated in total" source**, chaining study-publication bias → outcome-reporting bias → spin → citation bias.
45. **Stoll et al. 2020 spin study full text** — paywalled; the 76% / 94% figures are `?`.
46. **Fernandez et al. 2015 dropout meta-analysis full text** — abstract only.
47. **A completed (not protocol) Campbell review on CBT and reoffending**, testing the decline effect outside depression — only the 2024 protocol exists (Cook W. et al., *Campbell Systematic Reviews* 20(3):e1425, 2024-07-31), which also flags a **"scale-up penalty"**: *"measures of effectiveness drop considerably when an intervention moves from a demonstration project to large-scale delivery across a service."*
48. **Johnsen & Thimm 2018 (group CBT) and Thimm & Johnsen 2020 (MBCT) decline analyses** — cited only second-hand via that protocol.
49. **Any HAS document dated after 2017 that evaluates the *efficacy* of TCC** (as opposed to listing reimbursable acts) — re-searched 2026-07-28; found only the October 2017 RBP, the January 2025 ALD n°23 actes-et-prestations guide (explicitly *"n'est pas une recommandation de bonne pratique"*), and the April 2026 avis on sick-leave référentiels.
50. **Cuijpers, "Has the time come to stop using control groups in trials of psychosocial interventions?", *World Psychiatry*** — cited as "in press"; publication status unconfirmed.
51. **Any direct replication (registered or otherwise) of a landmark CBT trial** — none found. Absence of evidence here may be evidence of absence, but the search was not exhaustive enough to assert it.
52. **The 2024 differential-therapist-effects paper** (psychodynamic 10–16% vs CBT lower) — surfaced but not read. It would matter for the claim that manualisation compresses therapist variance.
53. **The NHS Talking Therapies deprivation gradient** (`?` 52.4% recovery in the least-deprived decile vs 40.5% in the most-deprived, 2024/25) — the annual-report landing page does not carry it; it is in the data files or the dashboard.
54. **Cuijpers 2026 long-term meta-analysis RoB distribution** — not in the abstract; full text paywalled.
55. **A current Cochrane review of CBT for adult depression** — **none exists.** Cochrane appears to have ceded this ground to the Metapsy living review, which is a notable structural fact about the field's evidence governance but one that could not be confirmed.
56. **Berendsen 2024's finer evidence-class split** (`?` "7.1% suggestive, 35.7% weak") and the `?` "71/286 (24.8%)" ungradeable-comparisons figure — not in the abstract, unconfirmed.
57. **The Leichsenring "science or alchemy?" critique's journal of record, volume and DOI** — unidentified.

### 11.7 Unresolved from the digital / delivery research

58. **Is Rejoyn still actively marketed in the US in mid-2026?** Everything verifiable is launch-era. `rejoyn.com` and `rejoynhcp.com` resolve but returned no extractable body text; the FDA 510(k) database query returned HTTP 404. A 2026-dated blog asserting continued availability plus a "$99/month cash-pay relaunch", a "~45% week-3 discontinuation" estimate and a **De Novo DEN230063** authorisation was **rejected as an unacceptable source** (unattributed AI-style aggregator; its De Novo claim conflicts with the sponsor's own "cleared" language). **Where to look next:** the FDA 510(k)/Product Classification database on product code **PWE** and 21 CFR 882.5801/882.5803; Otsuka's newsroom and IR filings.
59. **Is any digital therapeutic for a mental-health indication actually reimbursed in France (PECAN or LPPR) as of July 2026?** The pathway is fully documented but **no official published list of PECAN-admitted or LPPR-listed mental-health DMN was found.** **Where to look next:** the LPPR on ameli/Légifrance; the HAS CNEDiMTS avis database filtered on "DMN"; the G_NIUS registry; JO arrêtés d'inscription citing art. L. 165-1-1 CSS.
60. **The PECAN tariff.** `?` The figures €435 TTC / 3 months, then €38.30 TTC/month, €780 TTC/year cap, attributed to an "arrêté du 22 avril 2024", **appear only in commercial explainers and were not verified against the arrêté**.
61. **Nevada and Utah AI-therapy statutes** — confirmed only via law-firm and APA-Services summaries; the statutes themselves were not read.
62. **Whether the FDA has issued any post-DHAC policy output** on generative-AI mental-health devices since 2025-11-06 — the Federal Register notice was blocked by an anti-scraping CAPTCHA.
63. **A post-2020 independent replication of Baumel's real-world retention benchmark** — see §11.1 item 10.
64. **NHS England's stated reliable-recovery targets** (51% by 2026/27, 53% by 2028/29) — sourced only from a Nuffield Trust summary.
65. **Whether psychologists are legally within the *télésoin* category in France.** HAS's télésoin work covers pharmacists and *auxiliaires médicaux* (18 professions since 3 June 2021); psychologists are neither, and Mon soutien psy remote sessions run under their own code (PSS) and convention — **but no text explicitly excluding psychologists from télésoin was found.** HAS's télésoin doctrine is nonetheless directly relevant: *"la vidéotransmission est un critère déterminant"*, a phone call is not a télésoin, *"le premier soin aura lieu autant que possible en présentiel"*, and decisively **"une prise en charge exclusivement en télésoin n'est pas recommandée"** (HAS, February/March 2021).
66. **Head-to-head "chatbot-delivered CBT vs guided iCBT" trials** — **none found. This is the single most decision-relevant missing comparison in the digital section.**
67. **Tong 2026's four missing CAU-referenced point estimates** (no support, support on demand, minimal coaching) — they sit in Figure 3 and the Table 2 league table, which are image/table objects absent from the HTML text layer. ⚠ An automated summariser produced values for them **and also fabricated a with-contact effect range that appears nowhere in the article**, so **nothing from that summary is used**. Also missing: the with-contact subgroup point estimates, and the **CINeMA/GRADE certainty ratings per comparison** — without which **no statement of the form "moderate-certainty evidence that…" can be made about any specific contrast in that NMA**.
68. **Whether any body has yet cited Tong 2026 in guidance**, and whether any published commentary or letter has responded to it — none found; the paper was ~9 weeks old at the research date.

### 11.8 Unresolved from the guidelines research

69. **NICE CKS insomnia (2025) primary page** — NICE's own GID-HTE10068 project text cites "NICE CKS, 2025" as recommending CBT-I first-line for chronic insomnia at any age (that is the sourced claim used here); the CKS page itself returned CRAWL_NOT_FOUND.
70. **Whether the HAS 2006/2007 insomnia RBP is formally flagged obsolete or archived** — the page shows no obsolescence banner but HAS does archive old RBPs; **not confirmed either way**.
71. **HAS recommendation for ADHD in adults** — a secondary French source said adult recommendations were "expected by end of 2025"; not verifiable against has-sante.fr.
72. **Timeline and scope of the APA depression-guideline update panel appointed in 2025** — APA states the panel exists but publishes **no expected date or PICO scope**.
73. **NICE CG91 (Depression in adults with a chronic physical health problem, 2009) current status** — NG222 cross-references it as live, but its own surveillance status was not checked.
74. **Whether NICE CG31 is described anywhere as partially replacing CG123** — a search summary said so; the CG31 overview page does not.
75. **Full verbatim text of the HAS TDAH recommendation on TCCE** (section 3.3.2, p. 122 of the argumentaire) — the PDF returned undecodable streams. **Consequence: no grade for any TDAH intervention is currently sourceable, and a previously asserted "grade B" for psychoéducation has been removed as unsourced.**
76. **European (ESRS) insomnia guideline 2023 update** — referenced by a non-primary conference-report site in one file; the primary citation (Riemann et al., *J Sleep Res* 2023;32(6):e14035) is used elsewhere in this dossier.
77. **Australian/NZ (RANZCP) mood-disorder CPG revision status, and ACP's 2023 depression guideline** — not searched.
78. **The exact day in October 2025 of the NG222 rec 1.5.3 amendment** — NICE's update page is month-granular. Low value; the month suffices.
79. **The surveillance decision behind NG222's "Last reviewed: 30 January 2026"** — linked but not retrieved (though its trigger and reasoning are documented in §8).
80. **Whether the October 2025 NG222 amendment touched anything besides rec 1.5.3** — NICE names only 1.5.3, and December 2025 separately names tables 1 and 2, so the two edits are distinct on NICE's own account; `?` a full text-diff against the 2022 archive was not performed.

### 11.9 Unresolved from the France research

81. **EABCT minimum training standards** — see §11.1 item 16.
82. **The LFSS 2026 article number carrying the tiers payant generalisation** — see [§10 item 4](#10-where-this-dossiers-own-sources-disagree).
83. **Waiting time to obtain an appointment with a libéral psychologist in France** — **no public statistic exists**; DREES's survey explicitly excludes psychologists.
84. **Mutuelle / complémentaire coverage of psychotherapy outside Mon soutien psy** — no primary source found for typical forfait levels or prevalence; all hits were insurer marketing pages.
85. **Full text of décret n° 2026-163 du 4 mars 2026 on Légifrance** — would not open on re-check; corroborated by four independent records, **only the article text is unread**. Note a minor conflict: one professional association writes « publié au Journal officiel le 7 mars 2026 » against JO n° 56 du **6** mars 2026 in both catalogue records — 7 March is the entry into force.
86. **The 0-vs-2-month stage cell for psychologues** in the annexe of décret 2010-534, and the block-by-block split for *autres docteurs en médecine*.
87. **How many French TCC therapists there are, and what share of Mon soutien psy psychologists practise TCC** — **no source. Neither the CNAM nor the ameli directory records therapeutic modality.**
88. **A national count or registry of TCC DU/DIU programmes** — only individual university pages exist.
89. **The MIVILUDES 2022-2024 activity report's own figures on health-related signalements.** The MIVILUDES web page states ≈40% (undated); Psycom cites 25% for 2021; and Berna F. (*HEGEL*, 2025-06-19, DOI 10.3917/heg.152.0109) argues the 2022-2024 report **publishes no PSNC-specific figure at all**, does not define PSNC, and conflates *saisines* with *signalements*. `?` The MIVILUDES page also asserts "4 000 « psychothérapeutes » autoproclamés n'ont suivi aucune formation" — **undated and inconsistent with other official publications.**
90. **Whether AFTCC's or the Lyon/Chambéry DIU's training is *formally accredited* by EABCT as a course** (as opposed to AFFORTHECC being accredited as an association in 2017). The Lyon DIU says only « proche des critères ».
91. **Whether psychotherapy still has no dedicated act in the French nomenclature** beyond group therapy — sourced only to INSERM 2004; not re-verified against the current NGAP/CCAM.
92. **The primary ministry dossier de presse for the 11 June 2025 plan « Repérer, soigner, reconstruire »** — reported from a specialist trade outlet; the ministry PDF was not fetched.
93. **Reconciliation of the two June-2026 government figures for Mon soutien psy** — see [§10 item 19](#10-where-this-dossiers-own-sources-disagree). **Where to look next:** CNAM's *Rapport charges et produits*, or the évaluation report mandated by article 79 LFSS 2022.

### 11.10 Unresolved from the frontier research

94. **FDA's primary AR/VR medical-device list and the behavioural-health share of it** — the FDA page returned HTTP 404; only a consultancy summary reports the 2025-12-05 update to 104 entries, **rejected as a basis**.
95. **US regulatory status of gameChange / VRGC-940** — a US-facing Instructions-for-Use document uses prescription-device language, but **no 510(k) or De Novo number was found**.
96. **PETRUSHKA trial results** — only the tool description and the Oxford project page; **no outcome publication**.
97. **Process-based therapy efficacy data** — see §11.2 item 6.
98. **Any measurement-based-care meta-analysis newer than 2021 covering psychotherapy** (rather than pharmacotherapy) — none found.
99. **The ketamine + psychotherapy systematic reviews of 2025–2026** — named only in a search-engine summary with no resolvable citation; **rejected as a basis**.
100. **WHO guidance 2024–2026 relevant to psychological therapies** (an mhGAP successor; the "World mental health today" 2025 report, ISBN 9789240113817) — referenced inside a bibliography but not fetched.
101. **Fate of Somryst / Pear Therapeutics** — Somryst (K191716) is still the legal predicate for Daylight, but whether the product is still marketed was not verified.
102. **Effect sizes in the two 2025 machine-learning-prediction systematic reviews** — full text timed out; abstract-level claims only.
103. **`?` GRADE ratings attributed to the JITAI meta-analysis** — the published abstract reports PRISMA + Cochrane RoB and **no GRADE at all**.
104. **`?` Whether the CY2027 PFS proposed rule touches the DMHT codes G0552–G0554** — the CMS fact-sheet section fetched did not mention DMHT and the 1,592-page rule was not read. **Do not assert CY2027 DMHT policy from this dossier.**
105. **`?` Full statutory text of the 2026 state AI-therapy laws** (Maine, Colorado, Tennessee, Rhode Island) — enactment dates and summaries come from two trackers; unlike Illinois/Nevada/Utah, the primary texts were not fetched. **This is the single largest remaining sourcing weakness in the AI-regulation material.**

## 12. Provenance

This dossier was condensed from a thirteen-file raw research corpus — nine angle files and four gap-closure files — each of which carried its own *Current state of knowledge*, *Superseded / abandoned / contested*, *Unresolved*, *Source ledger* and *Verification log* sections. Every angle was adversarially re-verified against primary sources on 2026-07-28, and every one came back **CORRECTED**: the verification pass removed six fabricated "verbatim" quotations, reversed one meta-analysis that had been read backwards, and replaced a section built on a network meta-analysis whose literature search stopped in January 2019.

**That raw layer has been deleted.** What survives of it is what was folded into this dossier: the inline citations, the superseded register in §9, the contradiction register in §10 and the gap list in §11. The per-file source ledgers and verification logs are gone.

**To recover it**, check out the `me` repository at commit `15e599a` and read `reference/health/tcc/raw/` — for example `git show 15e599a:reference/health/tcc/raw/guidelines.md`. The table below records what each deleted file covered, so you can tell which one to recover.

**Consequence for use.** A claim in this dossier can no longer be traced to a research file's ledger; trace it to the primary source in its own inline citation instead. Where §10 says two corpus files disagreed, the underlying files are no longer here to consult — take §10's stated resolution, or re-verify at the primary source.

| File | What it covers |
|---|---|
| `foundations-models.md` | Foundations, waves and theoretical models — definitions, Beck's generic cognitive model, the three-waves dispute, process-based therapy and the EEMM, network models, the exposure mechanism story, and whether cognitive change is the active ingredient. |
| `techniques-protocols.md` | The clinical toolkit as actually delivered in 2026 — exposure, ERP, cognitive restructuring, behavioural experiments, behavioural activation, problem-solving, psychoeducation, relapse prevention, transdiagnostic protocols, modular vs manualised delivery, and session-dose specifications for England and France. |
| `evidence-by-disorder.md` | Disorder-by-disorder evidence base — the two cross-disorder anchors, then depression, GAD, panic, social anxiety, specific phobia, OCD, PTSD, insomnia, eating disorders, psychosis, substance use, chronic pain, health anxiety, adult ADHD, bipolar disorder and paediatric presentations, each with current effect sizes and first-line status. |
| `third-wave.md` | Third-wave and adjacent therapies — ACT, DBT, MBCT, mindfulness-based interventions and their harms, schema therapy, metacognitive therapy, CFT, FAP, behavioural activation, rumination-focused CBT — plus the EMDR-vs-TF-CBT guideline divergence and the "distinct or repackaged?" question. |
| `guidelines.md` | Current official clinical guidelines and their revision status — NICE (NG222, NG116, CG113, CG159, CG31, CG178, the digital-technology guidance), HAS, APA, WHO mhGAP 3rd edition, INSERM 2004, VA/DoD, CANMAT and Germany's NVL, with dates, tags and update timetables. |
| `efficacy-controversies.md` | How good TCC really is — the decline effect and its rebuttals, publication bias, the waitlist comparator problem, risk of bias, spin and preregistration, the dodo-bird verdict, therapist effects, dropout, harms, durability, the treatment-prevalence paradox, and for whom CBT does not work. |
| `digital-delivery.md` | Digital, remote and low-intensity delivery — the seven-support-level iCBT taxonomy, iCBT vs face-to-face, apps, NHS England's pathway and product gates, US prescription digital therapeutics and Medicare coding, generative-AI chatbots and their regulation, Germany's DiGA, and France's remote-delivery rules. |
| `france-practice.md` | TCC in France — who may legally practise, TCC-specific training and the AFTCC "TP" label, the full "Mon soutien psy" rule set and its 2024–2026 changes, psychiatrist tariffs and ALD, access and waiting times, where TCC appears in French guidelines, and the French-specific controversies (INSERM 2004, autism, "paramédicalisation"). |
| `frontier-2024-2026.md` | The 2024–2026 frontier — process-based therapy, precision psychotherapy, single-session interventions, measurement-based care, combination and deprescribing, psychedelic-assisted therapy, ketamine, d-cycloserine, VR exposure, digital CBT and prescription DTx, generative-AI CBT and the state AI-therapy laws, JITAIs, and guideline updates in progress. |
| `gap-1.md` | Working file: the guided/unguided iCBT binary retired — Tong et al. (BJPsych 2026) superseding Karyotaki 2021, including the seven-level definitions, the acceptability table, the long-term reversal, the network's inconsistency problems, and a protocol-vs-paper deviation audit. |
| `gap-2.md` | Working file: NICE NG222 recommendation 1.5.3 — the October 2025 amendment hidden behind a `[2022]` tag, with the live text, the 2022 original, the amendment record, the precise diff and a corpus-wide sweep for the stale string. |
| `gap-3.md` | Working file: the ISTSS guidelines' 10 October 2018 evidence cut-off (RCT searches to 31 March 2018) and the post-2020 authorities — APA 2025, VA/DoD 2023, Phoenix Australia, NICE NG116, Cochrane CD006869 — that now independently carry the psychoeducation, relaxation and debriefing verdicts. |
| `gap-4.md` | Working file: NICE CG113's panic-disorder CBT dosing — every dose recommendation tagged `[2004]`, NICE's own patient-facing admission that the panic advice was never updated, and the three-way verification that **no anxiety-guideline update is scoped**. |

**Two standing warnings for anyone re-verifying this material.** `nice.org.uk` returns **HTTP 403** to some fetchers — the NICE text throughout this dossier was retrieved through a different fetcher, not from memory or search snippets. And **automated page summarisers fabricated content at least four times** during the corpus's construction (an Angelakis 2022 headline that does not exist in the paper; a Jover Martínez 2026 "verbatim" passage; a Cambridge Core with-contact effect range; a Carona 2023 conclusion that inverts the paper's actual one). **Read the primary source, not a summary of it.**
